#2343. Man Utd impress in draw against PSG but injury worries mount

Michael Carrick said he had to be careful with Mason Mount after the England midfielder handed Manchester United an injury scare in their friendly draw with Paris St Germain.

Mount has had a torrid time with injuries since signing from Chelsea in a £55m-deal three years ago, starting only 25 Premier League games for United in that time.

The 27-year-old has been successfully deployed by Carrick in a central midfield role during pre-season, leading to speculation that United may not make another expected signing in that department.

But Mount played only 18 minutes of Saturday’s 1-1 draw against the European champions in Gothenburg before being replaced by Scotland international Tyler Fletcher.

“He [Mount] got kicked. We just wanted to be careful and look after him,” Carrick, the United head coach, told reporters in Sweden.

“We’ve been lucky with injuries so far, so we wanted to be careful.”

Post-match video footage showed Mount walking freely on to the United bus without the aid of a protective boot or knee support.

Michael Carrick told VG Mason Mount was taken off as a precaution. Looked OK leaving stadium. pic.twitter.com/Uo0fBtgpAl

United went behind within two minutes at the Ullevi Stadium when Liverpool target Ibrahim Mbaye punished slack marking to put PSG ahead.

Bryan Mbeumo fired United level after 32 minutes following good work from Amad Diallo, the Cameroon forward’s third goal in two games after his match-winning double against Atletico Madrid.

United meet Leeds in a Dublin friendly on Wednesday before coming up against AC Milan and their former manager Ruben Amorim next Saturday.

United open their Premier League campaign at Hull on August 22.

“It was good game. PSG is good so we had to keep building fitness. I am happy to have other [players] back and we have to keep going.”

“It’s good for me. I enjoyed it. We need to build fitness, we have a big season ahead.”

“It’s a good game PSG are tough to beat and we got a draw and now think about the next game [against Leeds in Dublin].”

“We have two more games against Leeds a and Milan. We need to have big season and hope to win a trophy.”

All over in Gothenburg and it’s been a good match for Michael Carrick’s team. They looked sharper and quicker than their win over Atletico Madrid last week (what the manager wanted) and things are moving in the right direction. Two things to worry about, however, were the early exits of Mason Mount and Tom Heaton with injuries. They’ll especially hope the Mount knock isn’t anything mid or long term.

Nearly done and it’s been a good run out for United. They’ve looked the better, sharper side and, if anything, deserve to win. In mitigation, this is United fourth pre-season game to PSG’s second...

Fernandes has one thing on his mind when he gets the ball 25 yards out in the centre of the park - a shot. He unleashes the attempt, destined for the bottom-left corner, forcing Longoni, the sub goalkeeper, into a good save.

Two chances for United in quick succession. A lovely ball from the middle of the park by Zirkzee finds Dalot on the right. He chips the ball to the back post where one of the Fletchers (sorry, I am not sure which) heads for goal. His attempt is blocked and the ball, ultimately, falls at the feet of Lacey whose shot is blazed high over the bar.

There have been plenty of good things for Carrick to take from this match. The passing, while not of the standard of a cut-and-thrust Premier League match, has been good for the time of year, and there have been enough flashes of brilliance/class/invention etc to suggest that pre-season is moving in the right direction.

Lovely bit of play by United. A great pass with the outside of the boot from the inside-left channel by Tielemans finds Fernandes who plays a sidefooted shot that goes just wide.

Tielemans’s first bit of brilliance from the Belgian there.

Both sides losing possession a fair bit at the moment. Strong pre-season vibes right now.

Maguire has had a good game. He’s played a number of long passes from the back and looked assured. This time he’s up in the PSG half and decides to have a pop - his shot testing Safanov, who saves well.

Had there been VAR Safanov might have had something to answer for...he may have beaten Zirkzee to the ball but he flew in the air and jurt the United man in the process.

Anwway, there’s no VAR (hurrah, for those of you who consider it a modern-day evil) and it doesn’t matter.

Chance for United to go ahead and it comes from two things. (i) A long, hopeful ball from Fernandes that looks for Zirkzee (ii) Safanov rushing off his line and into no-man’s land. The ball comes loose and Jack Fletcher, instead of having a go at goal, with the keeper on the ground and out of the game, passes to Lacey whose shot is wide.

Tyler Fletcher loses the ball near the United half and it, ultimately, results in a PSG corner. From the set piece United defend well and snuff out all hint of danger.

PSG seeing more of the ball at the moment. They work the ball to the right and Amass brings down Mbaye.

From the resulting free-kick, in the final third, the French side whip in the ball but Mee is up ot the challenge and come to collect well.

Santos, Mbeumo, Shaw, Amad, Lacey are off

Fernandes, Jack Feltcher, Zirkzee and Amass are on.

A raft of changes for PSG sees Marquinhos and João Neves, among others, come on. More like it, in terms of first-team ‘names’, on the pitch for the European Cup champions.

United on top in this half, as they were in the first.

The paces quickens and the game is much better for it. Mbuemo drives toward the PSG box, he cuts inside from the right before unleashing a shot that is bound for the back of the net before a fine one-handed save by Safanov. Credit, too, for Maguire whose long ball from the back found Mbuemo brilliantly. Promising...

Lacey tries to play in Dorgu on the left but he’s asking too much of his team-mate. Wonder when the expected raft of changes will take place?

Slow start to the second half. This is very much pre-season-friendly pace.

Having seen Mount limp off in the first half, there’s more worry at the start of the second and Heaton is force off with what looks like a hamstrung injury. He dashed off his line to clear a ball and in doing so has tweaked something. Mee replaces him.

Back under way Gothenburg - there have been a number of changes and will bring them to you once I’ve worked out who has come on and who they’ve replaced.

A world record that still stands...(this was back in 1995).

And that is it for the first 45 minutes. PSG took the early lead, but from that moment on United have been the better side and deservedly are level through Mbueno’s goal.

There will be one minute of added time at the end of what has been a good first half for Man Utd.

Amad has a go from outside the box forcing the save from Safanov. He’s such a live wire and one of the best in his position in the top flight. Him or Antony? No contest, strange it took United a while to work that one out.

Yet more good work from the underrated Amad. He again closes down well on the right before winning back possession and cutting the ball back for Lacey. To think he was starring for Sunderland in the Championship while Antony was starting for United...

United have been the better side since conceding earlier and, as if to illustrate my point, as I typed that an Mbuemo free-kick is well saved by Safanov. That was heading for the top-right bin.

Great work on the left by Dorgu and Shaw ends with the latter chipping the ball to the back post where Amad is lurking with intent. The Ivorian looks to head the ball home, but before he can get anything on it it looks as though he’s pushed in the back. United players are sure it’s a penalty but, more importantly, the referee doesn’t.

Amad does brilliantly, winning the ball off Koukaba in the final third, from there he scampers toward the box before playing in Mbuemo who takes one touch before firing in a shot into the bottom right corner of the net.

Free-kick for PSG on the right, near the final third. The ball comes in and it’s straight into the arms of Heaton in the United goal.

Kvaratskhelia, not seen much of him so far, runs into the box, but Amad does well to usher him back out.

The match is pedestrian at the moment, but as I type that, Dorgu, yep, him again, drives down the left and puts in a good cross that is well intercepted by Safanov in the PSG goal.

United continue to see more of the ball, Dorgu’s seeing more of it than most. But, no chances really fashioned, still, plenty of time to go.

Worry for United having to sub off Mount. It looks as though he took a knock to a foot and Carrick and co will hope it’s not a long-term injury. Mount, who’s had a good pre-season, has been replaced by Fletcher, son of Darren.

Foul on Mazraoui results in two things. The Mount has to come off, and, United have a free-kick on the edge of the PSG area.

From the set-piece Mbuemo has a go and it’s a regulation save from Safonov in the PSG goal.

Heaven loses possession in the middle of the park and from there it’s like the parting of the Red Sea as PSG have a three-on-one. Mbaye gets the shot in on goal but Andrey Santos covers back brilliantly and does enough to get something on the ball. He’s started both this match and pre-season well.

United are looking half decent on the ball. They’re passing it well and not afraid to switch things around.

A lovely long ball from Maguire finds Dorgo whose lovely first touch takes him into the box before a cut back is intercepted by a PSG man. Promising signs though for Carrick’s men.

United seeing more of the ball at the moment. They’re being patient but cannot fashion an opening and the ball is worked all the way back to Heaton in goal.

Needless to say this is being played at a slowish pace...no blood-and-guts challenges being put in.

Good work down the right sees Amad plays in Shay Lacey who crosses into the box. The ball find shte head of Mbuemo and the ball sails over the bar. Better from United.

United started slowly against Atletico and they’ve done the same here.

Bright start to say the least from the French side. They work the ball left and it’s an easy cross from Dro Fernandez for Mbaye to tap home for the very early lead.

They’re under way in Sweden. United are in their new vibrant red kit and PSG in their blue, red and white kit. Big fan of the French side’s kit, it harks back to the kit they had in the 1990s when the likes of George Weah and David Ginola were entertaining in the capital of France.

Man Utd: Heaton, Mazraoui, Maguire, Heaven, Shaw, Mount, Santos, Dorgu, Lacey, Amad, Mbeumo; Subs: Heath, Mee, Amass, Armer, Dalot, Yoro, Fernandes, J. Fletcher, T. Fletcher, Tielemans, Zirkzee

Paris St-Germain: Safonov, Pacho, Zabarnyi, Koukaba, Boly, Beraldo, Dro, Mayulu, Kvaratskhelia, Ayari, Mbaye; Subs: Marquinhos, Mendes, Lucea, Vitinha, Neves, El Nay, Idder, Drame, Bourdin, Ndjantou, Meite, Longoni, Chevalier

And here’s a famous match that took place there, giving a certain Sir Alex Ferguson his first European trophy.

“Get a bit sharper, get better on the ball, go up a notch.”

“It’s not about today it’s about preparing. Some players have played 90 minutes, some have played three games, some have just come back. So it’s about keeping working on things we’ve been working on.”

Ready to face PSG in our latest pre-season test ✊

Manchester United continue their Sweden sojourn with match against Paris St-Germain in Gothenburg.

Clearly a clash against the back-to-back Champions League winners is a step up from their previous two pre-season friendlies (victories against Rosenborg and Atletico Madrid), but at the same time this is more a chance for players to get miles in the legs and see how new signings and possibly some young guns fit in.

One of those new signings is Youri Tielemans, and the Belgian international is in line to make his debut after his move from Aston Villa. Other team news is that Bruno Fernandes is back and ready to captain the team.

Michael Carrick has also been boosted by the returns of Matheus Cunha, Diogo Dalot and Noussair Mazraoui after their post-World Cup breaks.

Cunha, however, will not play a part in today’s match because of a “minor illness” according to the MUTV website. He is expected to link up with the squad in Ireland, where they face Leeds United at Croke Park, after the match.

This will be Man Utd’s first outing since the 2-1 win over Atletico, a performance and result that pleased Carrick.

“I enjoyed it, I thought there was a lot of good in things that we are looking to improve on,” the United manager said. “Building on what we did for the last seven months, I think there is a lot of good signs for us, it’s never going to be the exact stage we want to be at. But I thought there was really a lot of positives to take out of the game, individual, collectively as a team, senior players looking strong and fit, and the younger players doing really well.

“The spirit and the camaraderie is so important for the team, it’s great to see the boys getting on so naturally and enjoying each other’s company. Collectively we looked strong as a team, which is always the most important thing, and individual performances seeing the young players come on to have an impact.”

Kick-off is set for 4pm so stay here for the build up, team news and action.

#2342. Gennaro Gattuso premiato Allenatore del Mese di ottobre

Il tecnico biancoceleste vince il premio di Allenatore del Mese di ottobre assegnato dalla Lega Serie A

Dopo 10 giornate la Lazio di Gennaro Gattuso si trova in testa alla classifica in solitaria con ben 28 punti a fronte dei 30 disponibili. Gli ultimi 30 giorni sostanzialmente perfetti sono valsi al tecnico biancoceleste il premio di Allenatore del Mese di ottobre assegnato dalla Lega Serie A.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Il premio Philadelphia Coach Of The Month di ottobre è stato assegnato all’allenatore della Lazio Gennaro Gattuso. La consegna del trofeo avverrà nel pre-partita di Lazio-Lecce, in programma sabato 21 novembre alle ore 15.00 allo stadio “Olimpico” di Roma.

Il premio è stato assegnato da una giuria composta da Direttori di testate giornalistiche sportive che hanno valutato i singoli allenatori in base a criteri tecnico sportivi e di qualità di gioco espresso dalle loro squadre.

Per il calcolo finale sono state considerate le giornate dalla 6ª alla 9ª della Serie A Enilive 2025/2026.

“Il primato solitario in classifica con il record di punti dopo 10 giornate nella storia della Lazio raccontano l’impatto di Rino Gattuso sulla panchina biancoceleste - ha commentato l’Amministratore Delegato di Lega Calcio Serie A Luigi De Siervo -. Gattuso ha costruito una squadra moderna dotata di organizzazione, intensità, e soprattutto capace di segnare con dodici giocatori diversi e di difendere con la mentalità di chi non vuole concedere nulla. Al di là del valore sportivo, va riconosciuto al tecnico calabrese il grande merito di aver trasmesso ai suoi ragazzi il proprio spirito combattivo, plasmando un gruppo unito che ha sa adattarsi al meglio ai vari momenti delle partite”.

#2341. Casertana, Stellone e il mercato: “Qualche intervento mirato a gennaio”

Tre partite senza vittorie e una classifica che si è fatta meno comoda: la Casertana prepara la sfida al Casarano, valida per la 13ª giornata di Serie C, con il tecnico Roberto Stellone deciso a respingere ogni allarmismo. Nel frangente i falchetti hanno raccolto due pareggi e il ko sul campo del Giugliano, ma in conferenza stampa l’allenatore ha rivendicato fiducia nel gruppo e nelle prospettive stagionali.

Il primo messaggio è netto: “La classifica, sinceramente, non la guardo. Con la squadra che abbiamo e per come stiamo lavorando, il pensiero di doverci guardare alle spalle non mi sfiora minimamente. Non è presunzione, ma conoscenza del campionato e dei giocatori a disposizione”. Stellone rilegge le ultime uscite una per una: contro il Monopoli “una grande gara”, contro il Sorrento una buona reazione allo svantaggio, a Picerno una partita interpretata nel modo giusto. L’unica vera battuta d’arresto è quella di Giugliano: “Non parlerei di presunzione, ma di un calo di concentrazione”. E ancora: “Sull’impegno, invece, non ho nulla da rimproverare ai ragazzi”.

Contro il Casarano, che ha cambiato allenatore e sistema di gioco con Luciano Zauri in panchina, Stellone si aspetta una gara simile a quella con il Giugliano: “È una squadra che aspetta, concede il possesso e difende con un blocco basso. Dovremo essere intelligenti nel non concedere ripartenze e nel mantenere equilibrio mentre attacchiamo”.

Sul tema rotazioni il tecnico chiarisce che qualche punto fermo c’è: “Sugli esterni Vezzoni e Oukhadda rappresentano oggi le prime scelte, mentre davanti Manconi e Castelli sono due attaccanti sui quali faccio grande affidamento. Per il resto non sto facendo esperimenti”. L’obiettivo è un altro: “Più che trovare undici titolari fissi, sto cercando di individuare il giocatore che sappia fare da raccordo tra centrocampo e attacco”.

Capitolo De Lucia, finito nel mirino dopo il gol subito a Giugliano. Stellone lo difende: “Victor è un portiere forte, che conosco dai tempi di Frosinone, e non lo cambierei con nessun altro della Serie C. È un ragazzo serio, attaccato alla maglia e molto coinvolto nel progetto. Ho piena fiducia in lui”. A chi chiama in causa i cambi in difesa risponde con i dati: “Da quando sono arrivato siamo tra le squadre che concedono meno occasioni e meno tiri in porta agli avversari”.

Lo sguardo è già rivolto al futuro: “La Casertana, alla lunga, sarà davanti. Ci mancano ancora alcuni passaggi per diventare la squadra che vogliamo essere, ma sono molto fiducioso. Con il lavoro e con qualche intervento mirato a gennaio nei ruoli in cui siamo numericamente più scoperti, possiamo crescere ancora tanto”.

In infermeria, l’unico da valutare è Belli, che ha accusato un piccolo affaticamento muscolare: “Decideremo con lo staff medico se sarà opportuno aggregarlo al gruppo o concedergli un turno di riposo. Per il resto stanno tutti bene”.

#2336. Arne Slot, l’anno sabbatico perfetto: quattro offerte rifiutate e il calcio vissuto da spettatore

Arne Slot ha scelto una strada diversa. Dopo l’esonero dal Liverpool, arrivato nel giugno scorso nello stesso giorno della finale di Champions League 2026, l’allenatore olandese non ha cercato subito una nuova panchina. Un anno dopo il trionfo in Premier League nella sua prima stagione ad Anfield, il tecnico ha deciso di prendersi una pausa dal calcio quotidiano e di cominciare una vera e propria tournée in giro per il mondo.

Slot ha collaborato con il gruppo degli osservatori tecnici UEFA durante le partite di Champions League, studiando squadre e producendo report tattici. Poi ha iniziato un viaggio nel mondo del calcio: è tornato a Rotterdam per assistere a una gara di Eredivisie del Feyenoord e successivamente è volato in Argentina per vivere dal vivo il Superclásico tra Boca Juniors e River Plate alla Bombonera.

Vestito con una maglia retrò di Diego Maradona, Slot ha assistito al successo del Boca per 1-0 in mezzo ai tifosi più caldi. Poco dopo si è spostato in Brasile, al Maracanã, per Flamengo-Gremio. Accompagnato dal figlio Joep, ha ricevuto una maglia personalizzata del club carioca.

La pausa, però, non significa mancanza di richieste. Slot avrebbe infatti detto no a diverse opportunità: Fulham, Milan e Al Ahli si erano interessati a lui, mentre anche la nazionale olandese aveva pensato al suo nome dopo l’addio di Ronald Koeman. Il tecnico ha preferito aspettare, spiegando di voler rimanere legato al lavoro quotidiano sul campo: "Un giorno sarebbe un grande onore allenare la mia nazionale, ma non è il momento giusto per me". Per ora Slot continua il suo particolare anno sabbatico: meno panchina, più calcio vissuto in ogni angolo del mondo.

#2334. Zazzaroni: “Nessuna frattura nello spogliatoio del Napoli, solo disorientamento da pressione negativa”

Ivan Zazzaroni, giornalista, è intervenuto a Radio 24: “Nessuna frattura nello spogliatoio del Napoli, c’è disorientamento da pressione negativa. Chiaro che quando le cose vanno male i giocatori non sono d’accordo con questo o quello, i giocatori hanno dato tutto per 60 minuti, purtroppo è poco”.

#2333. Provedel e l’Inter: «Un sogno che si realizza». Il ricordo di Toldo

Il portiere ammette l’incredulità iniziale e racconta la sua reazione da bambino al gol dell’idolo contro la Juve.

Ivan Provedel ha raccontato il suo trasferimento estivo dalla Lazio all’Inter nella quarta puntata di Welcome Kit, il format di Inter TV powered by Betsson Sport. Il portiere ha parlato della chiamata ricevuta dai nerazzurri, dell’obiettivo che si è dato nella nuova squadra e del ricordo di Toldo, il suo idolo.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Il contatto dell’Inter ha colto Provedel di sorpresa: «Quando mi ha chiamato l’Inter sono stato un po’ incredulo». A quella prima reazione non è seguita subito la certezza del trasferimento. Il portiere ha spiegato di aver aspettato che l’operazione fosse conclusa prima di lasciarsi andare all’entusiasmo: «fino a che non ho firmato non ho voluto pensare o illudermi, fino a che non è stato tutto fatto». Solo dopo la firma, ha raccontato, è stato felicissimo.

Provedel ha indicato anche quale sia il suo primo obiettivo da interista: «Dimostrare che qui ci posso stare e che posso dare il mio contributo alla squadra». Il portiere vuole quindi mostrare di poter far parte dell’Inter e aiutare i compagni. Alla domanda su che cosa rappresenti per lui indossare la maglia del club, ha risposto: «Un sogno che si realizza».

Nelle sue parole, l’attesa prudente prima della firma lascia spazio alla felicità per il passaggio all’Inter. Provedel ha collegato l’arrivo in nerazzurro al desiderio di dare un contributo alla squadra, oltre che al significato personale della maglia che ora indossa.

#2329. How long West Brom face without George Campbell as James Morrison confirms injury extent

West Brom host Millwall in the Championship this Saturday, and they will hope to extend their league unbeaten run to six games. However, WBA will have to do so without club captain George Campbell, who withdrew from the Southampton draw after feeling his groin.

Now, ahead of the Millwall tie, Baggies boss James Morrison has confirmed Campbell will be absent against the Lions. Speaking to the club’s official media, he said: “Unfortunately George picked up a groin injury against Southampton and we expect him to be out for between ten to 12 days. It’s not ideal, but it could’ve been worse. Hopefully it’ll just be one game that he misses.”

Campbell has played 15 times in all competitions this season, sitting unused on the bench on only one occasion. As captain, he serves as a key presence at centre-back, so while it is a blow to lose him, there will be relief that he isn’t out for an extended spell.

There’s no hiding that Campbell is a loss for Albion. He was instrumental in their survival turnaround last season and emerged as a real leader, earning the captain’s armband and retaining a key role for the 2026/27 campaign.

Thankfully for West Brom and Morrison, he has a straightforward selection decision when dealing with Campbell’s absence. Nat Phillips replaced him at the break on Tuesday night, and he will more than likely do so from the start against Millwall.

In the two Championship games Campbell has not started, it has been fellow centre-back Chris Mepham who has stepped in as captain. As such, he seems the likely candidate to do so against the Lions, although Jayson Molumby has donned the armband before as well.

Morrison will be hoping his remaining WBA centre-backs can avoid suspension or injury, as only Mepham and Phillips remain with Campbell sidelined.

Campbell was called up for the United States national team for their November fixtures. But, with the extent of his injury now confirmed, it will be expected that he withdraws.

That will give the West Brom man the break to recover from his groin issue and work back to fitness for the return to club football. WBA face Swansea City in the first game back, and the hope is that Campbell will be fit.

Morrison and the physios will be sure to be careful with Campbell to ensure he doesn’t suffer a further setback. Losing him for one game is a blow, but seeing him cast to the sidelines for longer would spark real concern given the Baggies’ lack of centre-back depth.

#2324. Birmingham City team news and predicted XI to face Swansea City: August Priske remains a doubt

Birmingham City welcome Swansea City to St Andrew’s this weekend looking to strengthen their bid for automatic promotion. The Blues currently sit 3rd in the Championship table with 26 points from 14 games, level with Stoke City and just two points behind leaders Middlesbrough.

Chris Davies’ side head into the contest in excellent form after a convincing 3-0 win over Millwall last time out. Alex Cochrane, Max Bird and Jay Stansfield all got on the scoresheet in the first half, while summer arrival Liam Millar has also made an impact with four goals to his name.

Swansea City are also firmly in the promotion hunt, occupying 5th place with 26 points from their opening 14 fixtures. Vitor Matos has enjoyed an encouraging start to his tenure in South Wales, with the Swans demonstrating plenty of quality throughout the campaign.

However, the visitors will be keen to bounce back after suffering a damaging 3-1 defeat away at Portsmouth. Swansea looked to have rescued a point after fighting back from a goal down, but two goals in stoppage time, arriving in the 92nd and 98th minutes, condemned them to defeat.

August Priske is a major doubt for Birmingham City’s Championship clash with Swansea City this weekend, with the striker set to be assessed on Friday before a decision is made on his availability, as reported by Birmingham Live.

The Dane missed Tuesday night’s 3-0 victory over Millwall after picking up an injury during the previous weekend’s meeting with Lincoln City. Priske has scored seven goals this season and would be a significant absence for Davies’ side.

The Blues boss provided a positive update but admitted it remains too early to know whether his leading scorer will feature. He said: “He’s doing OK. He’s made progress. It was a heavy knock that he took and he’s made progress but it’s probably too early to say.”

Davies added that it would be a close call, with Birmingham hoping to get Priske involved in some capacity. Paik Seung-ho and Dael Fry could return to the squad, while goalkeeper James Beadle is expected to miss out again.

Allsop; Laird, Neumann, Klarer, Cochrane; Solis, Iwata; Roberts, Bird, Millar; Stansfield

After the performance against Millwall in midweek will likely see Davies keep the same team. The only change may depend on Priske’s fitness.

Should Priske be fit then Bird will likely drop out of the side with Stansfield sitting deeper. However, both Bird and Stansfield impressed in midweek so it would be harsh to drop them.

Ryan Allsop will keep his place between the sticks with Beadle remaining out. While Fry and Paik will start from the bench should they return to the squad.

#2323. Nesta e i derby: "A Roma ho fatto i danni, soffrivo troppo. A Milano giocavo in scioltezza"

L'ex capitano biancoceleste racconta le forti emozioni vissute nelle stracittadine del passato e presenta il derby contro il Benevento sulla panchina dell'Avellino

Alla vigilia del sentito derby di Serie B tra Benevento e Avellino, l'allenatore biancoverde Alessandro Nesta fa il punto sulle sue emozioni. Mettendo a confronto la sfida con la sua celebre esperienza vissuta nella capitale, l'ex capitano biancoceleste spiega come il derby contro la Roma rappresentasse per lui una grandissima pressione e una fonte di ansia immensa, vissuta ben diversamente rispetto alla tranquillità del derby tra Inter e Milan.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

I derby? In quelli di Roma ho fatti i danni. Essendo la mia città lo soffrivo troppo. Nessun'altra partita, neppure la finale di Champions League, mi ha mai trasmesso una simile ansia. Quelli di Milano li ho giocati in scioltezza. Uno l'ho perfino giocato in occasione di una semifinale di Champions, ma il giorno dopo ero in bicicletta a Corso Vercelli. Un altro mondo.

Da allenatore il derby lo sento. Non come quello di Roma, ma lo sento. So che è una partita importante. So come si sente la gente, perché sono stato anche tifoso. So che dobbiamo andare a fare una partita vera. Trasferta vietata? I derby devono avere entrambe le tifoserie. A noi mancherà qualcosa, ma questo deve darci forza. Dobbiamo andare a lottare: per noi e per loro, che non potranno esserci.

#2321. Sheffield United team news and predicted XI to face Charlton Athletic: Joe Rothwell remains out as key man returns

Sheffield United head into Saturday’s clash with Charlton Athletic after an impressive victory over Preston North End at Deepdale. Sydie Peck stole the headlines with a first-half hat-trick as SUFC secured a 3-2 win on Tuesday night.

Making their second successive away trip, Chris Wilder’s side will be keen to build on that result and maintain their recent momentum. The Blades have won three of their last five matches and are averaging 1.57 points per game this season.

Their opponents, Charlton Athletic, go into the fixture following a disappointing 1-0 defeat against Bolton Wanderers. CAFC are now winless in their last 11 matches, collecting just five points since September.

However, returning to The Valley could offer some encouragement for Nathan Jones’ side. The Addicks are averaging 1.29 points per game on home soil this season and have conceded fewer than a goal per match in front of their own supporters.

Sheffield United will welcome back Kalvin Phillips for Saturday’s trip to Charlton Athletic, but fellow midfielder Joe Rothwell is set for a spell on the sidelines after undergoing a minor groin operation, as reported by The Sheffield Star.

Rothwell picked up the problem during the warm-up before last Friday’s win over Wrexham and is expected to miss the upcoming fixtures against Watford and Southampton. Wilder confirmed he should return after the international break.

Phillips, meanwhile, missed the victory at Preston North End with an ankle injury but has recovered and is available for selection. The Manchester City loanee was also named Sheffield United’s player of the month for October after helping the Blades build momentum.

Patrick Bamford remains a doubt, with the striker facing a late fitness test on the shoulder injury he sustained against Derby County. Wilder confirmed the forward has returned to training but must prove he can cope with physical contact before being cleared to play.

Cooper; Choudhury, Tanganga, Kitching, Burrows; Chong, Peck, Riedewald, Donovan; Brereton Diaz, Campbell

Despite the win over Preston, changes may be afoot from SUFC against Charlton Athletic. Despite that Phillips will likely take a place on the bench.

Given his injury record, Wilder will not want to take any risk with Phillips. Peck and Jairo Riedewald both remain in midfield following the midweek win.

The two changes come in attack and at the centre of defence with Tyrese Campbell and Liam Kitching return to the side. Campbell comes in for Tom Cannon alongside Ben Brereton Diaz, while Kitching is in for Mark McGuinness after he served his suspension in midweek.

#2318. Napoli-Lazio, le probabili formazioni: chance per Giovane. Pinamonti torna titolare

Le ultime dai campi e le probabili formazioni di Napoli-Lazio:

Emergenza totale per Massimiliano Allegri in vista della sfida contro la Lazio, capolista del campionato. Dopo la pesante sconfitta per 4-1 in Champions League contro il Porto, il tecnico azzurro è chiamato a ridisegnare la formazione, complici le numerose assenze e gli ultimi problemi fisici che hanno ulteriormente ridotto le possibilità di scelta. A complicare la situazione sono soprattutto le condizioni di Giovanni Di Lorenzo e Mathias Olivera, entrambi fermatisi a Torino. A loro si aggiunge la squalifica di Marianucci, espulso contro la Juventus. La formazione dovrebbe prevedere Meret tra i pali, con una linea difensiva composta da Beukema, Rrahmani e Rafa Marin. Sulle corsie esterne spazio a Politano e Lang, mentre in mezzo al campo Anguissa, Gilmour e McTominay rappresentano le principali soluzioni. De Bruyne dovrebbe agire alle spalle di Hojlund, pronto a tornare titolare. Restano però due ballottaggi: Lang è favorito su Vergara con il 60% delle possibilità, mentre Gilmour parte avanti rispetto a Giovane, con un 70% contro il 30%.

È costretto a cambiare Gennaro Gattuso e lo fa in attacco per l’infortunio di Mattia Zaccagni. Il capitano biancoceleste non ci sarà a Napoli per una lesione muscolare. Al suo posto toccherà ad Andrea Pinamonti, che torna titolare dopo due mesi al centro dell’attacco con lo spostamento di Noslin a sinistra nel tridente completato da Gustav Isaksen. Per il resto nessuna novità nonostante i ritorni tra i convocati di Rovella e Marusic. Davanti a Mandas linea difensiva a quattro con Sutalo e Provstgaard al centro, Floriani Mussolini a destra e Nuno Tavares a sinistra, recuperato da un leggero affaticamento in settimana. A centrocampo nona consecutiva da titolare per Belahyane in regia, con Frattesi e Taylor inamovibili ai suoi lati a completare la mediana.

#2316. Scott Parker can make early West Ham statement by entrusting 19-year-old with starting role after Cardiff City impact: Opinion

West Ham started the Scott Parker era in dramatic fashion, as they came from 3-0 down to draw 3-3 away at Cardiff City. WHUFC’s new boss did not hesitate to make changes at the break in the bid to inspire a turnaround either.

Parker made three substitutions at half time, with Joel Piroe replacing Taty Castellanos, Mohamadou Kante coming on for Soungoutou Magassa and youngster Ezra Mayers taking Morato’s place at left-back.

19-year-old Mayers is the reigning Academy Player of the Year and has been around the first-team for much of the season. This was his first Championship appearance though, and Mayers’ impact has given Parker food for thought ahead of the clash with Blackburn Rovers.

Morato has been solid at left-back for West Ham. He has offered a more defensive presence while the starting right-back – usually Kyle Walker-Peters – is given more freedom to progress and link up with star player Jarrod Bowen in attack.

The Brazilian struggled on Wednesday night though, conceding a penalty as Jack Moylan ran riot. When deciding to replace Morato at the break, Parker had two academy graduates to pick from: the more experienced Ollie Scarles, or Mayers.

He went with the latter, and was rewarded for his faith. Mayers offered a front-footed and aggressive approach in his defending as he looked to win the ball back and set the Hammers on their way. The youngster made one tackle and one block, winning one ground duel and even volleying a late chance past the far post (all stats via FotMob).

Watching Mayers, he showed the hunger and desire that WHUFC fans want to see from players – just like new boss Parker did when he was running the show in the Hammers’ midfield.

Parker’s appointment has pleased the majority of supporters. Speaking to fans, you may hear them say ‘He’s West Ham’ – the energy, aggression and passion he played with in the midfield made him a firm favourite and a real standout, winning their Player of the Year award for three consecutive seasons from 2009 to 2011.

WHUFC fans are eager to see Parker manage in a similar way by setting up his team to be intense and fast-paced. Mayers’ substitute appearance showed just this against Cardiff City, and these attributes could see him force a way into the plans.

Pardon the cliche, but Parker ‘gets it’ at West Ham – he understands what it means to play for the club and knows what the fans want from those donning claret and blue. In his brief Cardiff appearance and academy displays, Mayers has shown this too.

By putting faith in Mayers and handing him a first league start for the club, Parker would be making a popular move that could set the tone for his tenure.

#2315. Napoli in ritiro, scelta presa da De Laurentiis: il patron è preoccupato

Francesco Modugno, inviato di Sky Sport, ha spiegato come il ritiro anticipato nasca dalla volontà della proprietà di favorire un confronto diretto tra i calciatori. L’obiettivo è individuare soluzioni concrete per uscire da una crisi certificata anche dai risultati. Queste le sue dichiarazioni: “La notizia è la scelta della volontà della proprietà di mandare la squadra in ritiro con un giorno di anticipo rispetto a quello che sarebbe stato, in ritiro per stare insieme e continuare a trovare soluzioni in un momento complicato e di crisi. Il presidente De Laurentiis è evidentemente preoccupato, da lì la scelta nella speranza che questo gruppo, parlandosi e dicendosi tutto nel faccia a faccia di domani, possa trovare le soluzioni e la forza in un momento difficile certificato dai numeri. Squadra in ritiro da domani: faccia a faccia, allenamento e tutti in ritiro”.

#2313. Convocato Jacopo Landi per la Coppa del Mondo Under 17

Gli Azzurrini faranno il loro esordio venerdì 20 novembre contro la Giamaica, per poi affrontare la Costa d'Avorio e infine l'Uzbekistan

Prima della partenza per Doha, la Nazionale Italiana di Daniele Franceschini si prepara in vista del Mondiale Under 17 in Qatar. Gli azzurrini, dopo la partecipazione ai Giochi del Mediterraneo e il successo nel torneo disputato in Croazia, si ritroveranno dal 12 al 15 novembre al Centro di Preparazione Olimpica Giulio Onesti dell'Acqua Acetosa, a Roma.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Nei 21 calciatori convocati, è presente anche il giocatore della Lazio Jacopo Landi. Secondo quanto riportato da La Gazzetta Regionale, l'Italia, reduce dal titolo europeo conquistato il 7 giugno e dalla medaglia di bronzo ottenuta nella precedente edizione mondiale, è stata inserita nel Gruppo E, insieme a Giamaica, Costa d'Avorio e Uzbekistan.

Gli Azzurrini faranno il loro esordio venerdì 20 novembre contro la Giamaica, per poi affrontare la Costa d'Avorio e infine l'Uzbekistan. Il torneo coinvolgerà 48 Nazionali suddivise in dodici gironi da quattro squadre. Accederanno alla fase a eliminazione diretta le prime due classificate di ciascun gruppo e le otto migliori terze. La fase a eliminazione diretta inizierà il 30 novembre con i sedicesimi di finale, mentre la finale per il titolo mondiale è in programma domenica 13 dicembre allo Stadio Ahmad Bin Ali di Doha.

La partenza per il Qatar è fissata per lunedì 16 novembre, quando il gruppo di Franceschini entrerà nella fase conclusiva dell'avvicinamento al Mondiale.

#2312. Middlesbrough team news and predicted XI to face Watford: David Strelec latest amid Will Lankshear concerns

Middlesbrough remain top of the Championship table despite suffering a disappointing 2-0 defeat away at Wrexham on Wednesday night. Kim Hellberg’s side missed the opportunity to extend their advantage at the summit but remain firmly in the promotion picture.

Boro are currently one point above Wolves in second place and two clear of Birmingham City, Stoke City and Swansea City. They were without key striker Will Lankshear in North Wales due to injury and struggled to find the firepower needed to get a result at the Racecourse Ground.

Watford, meanwhile, have endured a difficult start to the Championship season under head coach Alessio Dionisi. The Hornets sit 20th in the table after 14 fixtures and are just four points clear of the relegation zone.

However, they secured a much-needed victory last time out, beating Lincoln City 1-0 at Vicarage Road. Summer signing Stephy Mavididi scored the decisive goal to give Watford a welcome boost.

Hellberg will assess David Strelec’s fitness ahead of Middlesbrough’s trip to Watford on Saturday, but has hinted the striker may not be ready to star, as reported by Teesside Live.

Strelec made a positive impact from the bench during Wednesday’s defeat to Wrexham, offering better hold-up play during an 18-minute cameo. However, having only recently returned from injury, Hellberg believes starting him could carry too much risk.

The Boro boss said: “David has just come back and trained with us for only one week. We will see now, but, in general, I didn’t think he was fit enough, and there was too big of a risk to start him in a game now because of just two days in between.”

Lankshear remains hopeful of being available against Watford, although his absence was felt again on Wednesday as Kyle Joseph struggled to lead the line. Boro’s top-scorer also missed out on an England call-up due to his injury concerns.

Strelec’s campaign has already been disrupted by injuries, including a recent setback on international duty. Summer signing Rokas Pukstas and defender Alfie Jones remain out.

Vitek; Phillips, Malanda, Ayling; Brittain, Berhalter, Morris, Borges; Cozier-Duberry, McGree; Strelec

Two changes from the disappointing performance against Wrexham in the week. However, Lankshear once again misses out.

Strelec gets the nod in attack after his impressive cameo at the Racecourse Ground. That sees Joseph drop to the bench while Riley McGree returns to the side ahead of Tommy Conway.

#2308. Why West Brom’s £2m summer addition is becoming a smart signing under James Morrison

West Brom missed their chance to move into the top two as they drew 1-1 with Southampton on Tuesday night. As they prepare to host Millwall this weekend, James Morrison‘s WBA are serious about fighting for promotion this season, winning three of their last five including an impressive 3-1 win over West Ham at the London Stadium.

The start that they have had comes off the back of a successful summer window. Under Morrison, WBA made a real effort to revamp the squad with more youth and several new additions were made with that in mind.

Jimmy-Jay Morgan has taken a lot of the plaudits after all his goalscoring exploits. However, another one of their additions is starting to break into the side, Rabby Nzingoula.

The Baggies spent a reported £2m on the 20-year-old after a decent second half of the season on loan at Nurnberg. He has been a hugely important player over the past five games where West Brom have gone unbeaten.

The 20-year-old arrived from Strasbourg with plenty of potential but relatively little experience of English football. However, three months into his Hawthorns career, he is adapting to the Championship quicker than many would have expected.

Nzingoula has gradually moved from an impact substitute to a regular part of James Morrison’s midfield, making 14 appearances and accumulating 710 minutes. Seven of those outings have been starts, while his average rating of 6.73 suggests he is providing a dependable contribution despite his young age.

There is also more to his game than simply breaking up opposition attacks. Nzingoula has recorded 41 line-breaking passes and created nine chances in his Championship minutes so far. His 1.14 chances created per 90 puts him in the 75th percentile, while his shot attempts rank in the 80th percentile.

These figures suggest West Brom have a midfielder capable of contributing further up the pitch rather than simply protecting the defence.

At 20 years old, Nzingoula has a good level of experience at decent levels. The WBA man has played 37 times in Ligue 1 as well as featuirng in Europe for Strasbourg.

His versatility is another positive. Comfortable operating as either a six or an eight, Nzingoula gives Morrison different options in central and wider midfield areas. His recent performances, including a 7.3 rating against Southampton on November 3, suggest he is beginning to find his feet.

There are still areas for improvement. He has yet to register a goal or assist, his aerial numbers are relatively modest and three yellow cards highlight the occasional risks associated with his aggressive approach.

Nevertheless, West Brom should be encouraged. Nzingoula is only 20, has already demonstrated Championship-level defensive qualities and is showing signs of becoming a more complete midfielder. For a reported £2m, the Baggies could have secured a very smart long-term investment.

#2306. 2025 summer signing is slowly becoming a key midfielder for Millwall under Alex Neil: Opinion

Millwall have been a very dangerous side since Alex Neil was appointed boss. After narrowly missing out on promotion following a Championship play-off defeat to Hull City last term, the club are looking to bounce back once again.

Neil’s side have built a reputation for identifying players capable of making an impact in the Championship without spending excessively. This summer has seen Kyrell Lisbie come in and impress, while Lyndon Dykes has been an excellent free transfer.

While immediate impacts are always looked for with transfers, patience is also required sometimes. Derek Mazou-Sacko is becoming another example of that, and is showing that Millwall’s long-term recruitment working well despite a mediocre start to the campaign.

The French midfielder arrived from Rodez in August 2025 for a reported fee of around €500,000. There was little fanfare surrounding his arrival, but he has gradually established himself as an important part of Alex Neil’s side.

At just 22, he is already showing the qualities needed to become a reliable Championship performer. Mazou-Sacko is not a midfielder who will regularly grab headlines with goals, assists or eye-catching creative numbers.

His value lies in the less glamorous aspects of the game, and his latest statistics underline why Neil has increasingly trusted him. His aerial ability is another useful asset, standing at 6ft 1in, That physical presence helps Millwall compete for second balls and provides additional protection in front of their defence.

There is little suggestion that he should be expected to offer much more creatively. His five chances created place him in the 11th percentile, while his 440 touches rank in the 16th. However, that should not necessarily be viewed as a weakness.

He has completed 267 passes at an accuracy of 83.4%, including 46 line-breaking passes, showing he can retain possession and move the ball forward without taking unnecessary risks.

His development has been gradual. Having progressed through Troyes, spent time on loan at Nancy and subsequently made around 34 appearances for Rodez in Ligue 2, the move to English football represented an important opportunity.

He contributed to Millwall’s play-off push last season, making 23 league appearances and registering one goal and one assist. Now approaching 35 appearances for the club, he looks increasingly comfortable at this level.

Millwall will need more than one dependable defensive midfielder if they are to achieve their ambitions, but Mazou-Sacko is proving to be an excellent piece of business.

He might never become the most exciting player in Neil’s squad, but he makes the team more difficult to play through. Over a 46-game Championship season, that reliability can be just as valuable as an attacking match-winner.

#2305. Lesione all'adduttore per Giovanni Di Lorenzo

Il Napoli è stato impegnato in una seduta pomeridiana di allenamento nella quale ha preso parte anche Olivera

Secondo quanto raccolto dalle ultime notizie di Alfredo Pedullà, Giovanni di Lorenzo ha riportato una lesione all'aduttore. Il Napoli, in vista della prossima sfida contro la Lazio, è stato impegnato in una seduta pomeridiana di allenamento nella quale ha preso parte anche Olivera.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Gli azzurri si sono allenati in vista del prossimo match di campionato, domenica alle 12:30 al Maradona contro la Lazio. Il gruppo è stato impegnato in una seduta pomeridiana svolgendo attivazione fisica ed esercitazione in fase di possesso. Chiusura di sessione con una partitina. Olivera si è allenato con la squadra. Giovanni Di Lorenzo si è sottoposto a esami strumentali, presso il Pineta Grande Hospital, che hanno evidenziato una lesione distrattiva del muscolo adduttore lungo della coscia sinistra. Il calciatore azzurro ha già iniziato l'iter riabilitativo.

#2304. Inter-Como, le probabili formazioni: Pio Esposito con Thuram. Fabregas con i titolarissimi

Dopo la bella reazione arrivata in Champions League contro il Feyenoord, l'Inter vuole evitare altri passi falsi in campionato e si prepara ad affrontare il Como di Fabregas anch'esso fresco di vittoria pesantissima in Europa. Dato il delicato impegno, sarà importante per Cristian Chivu non stravolgere i reparti. #2303. Andoni Iraola: Liverpool cannot ‘sustain the level’ I want

Andoni Iraola has conceded that Liverpool cannot currently “sustain the level” he expects after his side squandered a two-goal lead for the second match in a row.

In the Spaniard’s first game at Anfield since becoming manager, Monaco fought back to win 3-2 by virtue of Paris Brunner’s late glancing header seven days on from Leeds United’s 4-2 victory in Chicago, emphasising Liverpool’s alarming lack of depth, particularly in defence.

“We played a good first half, especially a good 30 minutes. Right now we probably don’t have much more, in terms of the levels required to play like this. They deserved to turn around the game.

“We won the first two pre-season games, but the last two we cannot sustain the level we want for all of the game. As soon as we made some changes, players start to get tired and we cannot sustain that level. So, we have work to do.”

Alex on target 🎯 pic.twitter.com/PT6AYrLxyz

Alisson, Virgil van Dijk and Cody Gakpo all made their first appearances and contributed to a lively first-half in which Iraola’s commitment to man-to-man marking and an intensive pressing structure were evident out of possession. With the ball in the first half they demonstrated the kind of slick passing that Bournemouth employed to carve opponents open on the counter-attack.

A nimble one-two between Ryan Gravenberch and Dominik Szoboszlai allowed the Dutch midfielder to set up Alexander Isak to score with a shot from 16 yards and the most expensive player in English football history laid claim to the second, too, when he seemed to hook in a rebound from Lukas Hradecky’s save while lying in the six-yard box.

Later he would graciously acknowledge that he and Florian Wirtz had connected with the ball simultaneously and he was happy to yield the credit to his team-mate.

Our second goal of the game, scored by Wirtz… we think 🤔 pic.twitter.com/FUzA363Oxw

At that point it seemed that victory would be a cakewalk but just before half-time Aleksandr Golovin caught Van Dijk out with a feint and late swerve to the right, tripping over the Liverpool captain’s clumsy hack. Despite a heavy limp, Golovin hammered the penalty past Alisson’s dive to make it 2-1 at the break.

Iraola rationed his early changes at the start of the second half to two but Liverpool could not regain their rhythm and Mika Biereth capitalised on a Gravenberch error to score the equaliser after battling to be first to the rebound when Giorgi Mamardashvili saved his first effort, stroking his effort through Wataru Endo’s legs.

Cue five substitutions as soon as that goal had gone in and a raft more followed, killing the remaining half-hour of all momentum and cohesion. By the time Brunner exploited a dozy mix-up between Milos Kerkez and Federico Chiesa about whose responsibility he was at a corner to flash his header past Mamardashvili, only two of the 22 starters were still on the pitch.

Liverpool: Alisson (Mamardashvili 46), Tsimikas (Kerkez 59), Van Dijk (c) (Chambers 59), Ndukwe (Endo 46), Frimpong (Ramsay 75), Gravenberch (Nyoni 59), Gakpo (Ngumoha 59), Wirtz (McConnell 59), Koumas (Chiesa 75), Isak (Muñoz 59)Substitutes: Morrison, Davies.Goals: Isak (16), Wirtz (29)Monaco: Hradecky, Nazinho (Mawissa 75), Dier (c) (Kehrer 75), S Sané, Vanderson (Teze 46), Golovin (Idumbo 75), Cabral (Soubeir 75), Camara (Benzahra 64), Detourbet (Coulibaly 64), Biereth (Brunner 80), Abline (Konaté 64).Substitutes: Assab, Kohn, Lienard, Nibombe, Touré.  Goals: Golovin (44 pen), Biereth (56), Brunner (88)

They played well in the first half, as they did against Leeds, but lost all shape and structure in the second half to lose 3-2.

Yellow card for Konate for clattering into Endo late. That’s the last of the action.

Liverpool 2 Monaco 3 (Brunner)  Brilliant near-post glancing header from a devilishly whippy, dipping corner. Kerkez doing his best Rip van Winkle impression.

As usual, the substitutions have killed the game. Watched Leeds vs RB Leipzig yesterday. They played two 60 minute matches which kept it more competitive for longer but still descended into a kickabout late on.

Mawissa, Kehrer, Idumbo and Soubeir have replaced Nazinho, Dier, Golovin and Cabral.

Monaco also made changes, four I think. Will catch up with then once they’re confirmed. Ramsey is into the game straight away tp stop Coulibaly’s shot.

Ramsay, Chiesa and Elliott are coming on for Szoboszlai, Frimpong and Koumas. Lovely applause for Elliott.

Konate sweeps a left-foot shot from the left of the box past the right post with Mamardashvili forced into a headlong dive. Liverpool have lost their defensive shape and intensity, allowing Monaco to punch holes through the lines with some direct running.

Nazinho gets round Frimpong down the Monaco left and cuts back the perfect pass to put it on a plate for Coulibaly who hammers a left-foot shot from the right of the D that Mamardashvili throws up his hands to block. That would have stung. Good save. A flag would have meant it wouldn’t have counted but even so Frimpong’s defensive work wasn’t good enough.

It’s been very scrappy since the changes but then Ngumoha pounces on to a loose ball, glides up the right and tries to play Koumas in behind the back four. Sadly, he overhits the pass which trickles out for a goal-kick. Cue an ovation and cries of ‘Rio! Rio!’

Three changes for Monaco with Detoubet, Abline and Camara departing for Benzhara, Coulibaly and Konaté.

Ngumoha has gone to the right wing and Koumas is playing through the middle as a false nine.

Liverpool make five changes with Gakpo, Van Dijk, Wirtz, Tsimikas and Isak departing while Munoz makes his ‘debut’ with Ngumoha, Nyoni, Chambers, McConell and Kerkez.

Liverpool 2 Monaco 2 (Biereth) Mistake by Gravenberch allows Golovin to play a pass into the left half of the box for Biereth. His first shot is well saved by Mamardashvili at full stretch but his tenacity allowed him to get to the rebound ahead of Endo, swivel and stick his second effort in to equalise.

Monaco overplay at the edge of the box, Detourbet, Biereth and Abline playing ‘after you. Claud’, instead of shooting.

A slick, short passing move between Gravenberch, Wirtz and Isak ends with Isak on the deck in the box after Gravenberch’s step-over allowed the ball to run to him. Isak took a touch to move the ball on to his left but gave the defender a glimmer of it and he hacked it away before felling Isak as well as collateral damage.

Quiet start to the half. Biereth just pressed Mamardashvili but the keeper managed to sweep the ball away even though under more pressure than he would have expected.

Liverpool try to play out from the back with Mamardashvili spearing a straight pass to Gravenberch who tried to knock it round the corner for Endo but the ball skidded under his studs and out for a throw-in.

Isak is apparently claiming both goals. Replays are indistinct. He could have clipped it in off his heel as he had a swing at the ball while grounded at the same time as Wirtz.

Endo has slotted in at centre-back alongside Van Dijk.

Half-time changes: Mamardashvili for Alisson and Endo for Ndukwe.

For Monaco, Teze for Vanderson at right-back.

Much to admire in that first-half performance from Liverpool’s front six and centre-backs, the latter two particularly out of possession and winning it back. The man-to-man system is working well for them. But they should be out of sight by now instead of leading by only a goal.

Liverpool free-kick on the right after a foul on Koumas. Szoboszlai whips in the cross and Van Dijk gets up first to the left of the penalty spot but bludgeons his header about a foot wide of the right post.

Charlie Adam says the penalty was ‘a soft one’ but Van Dijk did not adjust when Golovin shifted it to his right, stuck out a leg and Golovim understandably decided to topple over the hurdle.

The referee, Jarred Gillett, gives Monaco a penalty. It was deserved, too. Golovin went on a driving run down the middle, shifted it to his right and was tripped by Van Dijk.

Liverpool are well worth their lead. They are streets ahead of Monaco.

A microcosm of the game so far: Liverpool pass goes astray, a player wins it back – this time Gakpo – by snapping into a tackle before laying it off to his left for the unmarked Tsimikas to skim a low shot about a foot wide of the left post from 25 yards.

Monaco have done very little since that wee salvo in the opening minute but just now Abline was given too much space from distance but whacked his shot into the Kop.

Big cheer from the crowd for Hugo Ekitike

Gakpo meets it with a header back across goal to Isak whose first effort from a couple of yards is blocked but the ball gets stuck under Isak’s falling body and Wirtz is Johnny on the Spot to stab it in from a 18 inches as Dier tried to scramble it away.

He was offside in any case, it transpires.

Charlie Adam on co-comms points out that Liverpool are going man-to-man now in classic Iraola/Bielsa style and when the camera goes high you can see it, especially with the full-backs.

He traps the chip, turns back to face away from goal and taps a pass back to Wirtz who gallops on to it and bends a shot that Hradecky dives low to his left to beat away with a firm left wrist. Tsimikas is free down the left but overhits his cross on the run when Isak was begging for a cut-back. The left-back went for the back post and ballooned his centre behind, miles beyond Koumas and Wirtz.

Liverpool defend the corner solidly and Frimpong races away on the counter, bombing on to the ball and sprinting into the Monaco half. But his touch is not as sound as his pace and Monaco deal with him when he fluffs his control.

A period of applause in memory of Kevin Keegan ❤️ pic.twitter.com/AkPZG7babe

Liverpool kick off defending the Kop. They roll the ball back to Frimpong who chips a long pass up the right to Koumas who hares on to the pass down the right and crosses, just beyond Isak. Hradecky gathers and bowls the ball out. A mistake by Tisimikas lets Biereth barrel through the inside-right to hammer a shot that Alisson turns behind for a corner.

The crowd applauds in the modern way. My goodness he was a terrific player. Saw him play for Liverpool, Southampton and Newcastle. Absolutely relentless.

Virgil van Dijk has the pennant. Liverpool are wearing all red with the paint splashes of the 1990 kit. Monaco are in a very pale green.

For me it’s a special game, most of the players and people will be ‘it’s [just] a pre-season friendly’, but for me it’s a lot more than this. It will be the first time I have the chance to go out, to feel this stadium and I hope we can also perform well.

It’s a good game for us, because it’s going to be demanding, it’s a team that wants to dominate and they also want to press high so we will see how we are.

I think the cases of Ali[sson], Cody, and Virgil I think it’s their first game. It shouldn’t go longer than 45 minutes, maybe a little bit more if they feel comfortable.

Also for some of the others Ryan, Flo, and Alex, it’s the second game so we have to be aware of those things.

But the league starts in two weeks, so we cannot wait also too much; we have to give them quality minutes.

I think today is going to be a good reference, two games – Monaco and Como – teams that try dominate the game, they are very good in possession, so they will demand us the best without the ball. If we want to control the game and also physical teams, they are probably a very good test.

I hope we are at the level and we can do very good games.

Alisson, Van Dijk and Gakpo return, Ndukwe continues in defence.

The Reds to take on AS Monaco at Anfield!Watch the match LIVE on All Red Video 📺

Good afternoon and welcome to live coverage of the first of two Liverpool home friendlies after their preseason tour of the United States. Andoni Iraola’s side saw off Wrexham and Sunderland in their first two matches but gave away a two-goal lead at half-time in the final fixture and ended up being beaten 4-2 by Leeds United in Chicago in the last game after multiple changes. What did we learn? Not a huge amount in the absence of Virgil van Dijk, Alisson, Víctor Muñoz, Cody Gakpo and Alexis Mac Allister, the first four of whom are available today other than an ongoing vulnerability from set-pieces and Curtis Jones looking like he is in desperate need of a fresh start.

This afternoon at Anfield they take on AS Monaco who finished seventh in Ligue 1 this season but are under new management with the recruitment of the former Chelsea and Atlético left-back Filipe Luís as head coach. It’s quite the coup for Le Rocher given he led Flamengo to their first Brazilian title in five years, the Copa Libertadores and the Copa do Brasil in his 18 months in charge.

In pre-season so far he has overseen a 5-2 win over fourth-tier AS Saint-Priest, a 2-0 defeat by Sporting Clube, a 2-2 draw with Cercle Bruges and a 1-0 victory over Getafe. They have sold their best player, Maghnes Akliouche, to PSG for £43m and Aladji Bamba to Newcastle for £34m but have so far retained Mamadou Coulibaly and Folarin Balogun, signed and made Ansu Fati’s loan move into a long-term deal.

Iraola may well persevere with Trey Nyoni in midfield who, after his performances on tour, ought to be on the threshold of a breakthrough season and it would be interesting to see him paired with Ryan Gravenberch just behind Dominik Szoboszlai.

Ifeanyi Ndukwe, who played well in America after coming on for the injured Joe Gomez, does not have a UK work permit and will have to go abroad on loan for the season but Jérémy Jacquet, who has yet to feature, was due to have a fitness test this morning and would be a welcome to boost to a squad whose defence still requires significant reinforcements.

#2302. Napoli, anche Manna è sotto esame: i motivi

In casa Napoli rischia di finire sotto esame anche il direttore sportivo Giovanni Manna. Lo annuncia La Repubblica, secondo cui il ds potrebbe essere chiamato in causa per rispondere all’emergenza infortuni che da mesi attanaglia la rosa a disposizione di Massimiliano Allegri. Era stato proprio l’allenatore del Napoli, infatti, a prendere le distanze dalle scelte di calciomercato operano in estate: “I problemi fisici dei giocatori? Basta conoscere il loro storico…”, aveva detto Allegri, come a voler addossare sulla direzione sportiva le responsabilità dei tantissimi infortuni. Potrebbe dunque essere proprio il ds Manna a pagare le conseguenze di tali scelte.

#2300. Free transfer signing is rediscovering his best form at Watford: Opinion

Watford have started this season poorly under Alessio Dionisi. Despite promising signs to start the year, Dionisi’s side have been stuck in a rut with led to talk of yet another managerial sacking from the Hornets.

Watford, who host Middlesbrough this weekend, need inspiration from somewhere as they look to climb away from the wrong end of the Championship table, and Myron Boadu could be providing exactly that.

The Dutch striker arrived at Vicarage Road on a free transfer in September after a difficult spell at PSV, but recent performances suggest he is beginning to rediscover the qualities that once made him such an exciting prospect.

His brace against Norwich City on October 31 was followed by an assist in the 1-0 victory over Lincoln City three days later. More importantly, these contributions have come alongside encouraging performances in other areas of his game.

Boadu is looking sharper, making intelligent runs and showing the work rate that made him so effective earlier in his career. There is a reason Boadu was once valued at more than €40m and attracted a €17m move to Monaco.

He scored 32 league goals in 64 appearances for AZ Alkmaar before enjoying another productive spell on loan at Bochum, where he registered nine goals in 19 Bundesliga appearances. Injuries and inconsistent opportunities subsequently disrupted his progress, but the underlying talent has never disappeared.

His early Watford numbers are encouraging. Across seven Championship appearances and 379 minutes, Boadu has scored twice and provided one assist, averaging approximately 0.7 goal involvements per 90 minutes, according to FotMob.

His 1.40 expected goals compared with two actual goals also suggests he is taking his opportunities well rather than relying on an unsustainable volume of chances.

There are encouraging signs beyond his attacking output, too. Boadu has registered five tackles, nine recoveries and 18 duels won, while recording 20 touches inside the opposition penalty area. These figures reflect a forward becoming increasingly involved in both Watford’s pressing game and their attacking transitions.

His developing partnership with Iker Bravo is another significant positive. Boadu’s understanding with the fellow forward appears to be growing, with their movement complementing one another without requiring extensive preparation.

The Norwich performance demonstrated their potential, while his contribution against Lincoln showed he can also influence games through his work without necessarily finding the net himself. Of course, seven appearances do not represent a complete revival, and Boadu still needs to deliver consistently over a longer period.

However, Watford cannot afford to overlook the encouraging signs. If he maintains this intensity and continues building his understanding with Bravo, the Hornets could have a valuable attacking weapon for the remainder of the campaign.

#2298. Haise difende Cahuzac dopo il 9-1: "So cosa significa subire una batosta in Champions"

La batosta contro il Como ha lasciato una ferita profonda in casa Lens. Il 9-1 subito in Champions League è diventato il risultato più pesante nella storia dei club francesi in Europa, ma Franck Haise ha scelto la strada della comprensione nei confronti di Yannick Cahuzac e della squadra.

L’ex allenatore dei Sang et Or, oggi sulla panchina del Rennes, ha parlato della pesante sconfitta in conferenza stampa: "La sconfitta è una cosa, perché conosciamo il livello di questa squadra del Como, ma è soprattutto l’ampiezza del risultato a fare la differenza. La Champions League è impietosa". Haise, però, ha ricordato che anche lui ha vissuto una serata simile sulla scena europea: "Provo empatia per Cahu, perché so cosa significa prendere una batosta in Champions League. Anche noi abbiamo perso 6-0 contro l’Arsenal".

Il riferimento è alla gara del novembre 2023, quando il suo Lens uscì travolto 6-0 dall’Emirates Stadium. Una sconfitta pesante, ma arrivata poche settimane dopo lo storico successo per 2-1 contro i Gunners: "La differenza è che tre settimane prima li avevamo battuti, quindi il sapore era un po’ diverso. Ma avevamo comunque preso sei gol. Per i giocatori, l’allenatore e lo staff sono momenti molto difficili. Non c’è altra soluzione che tornare a combattere".

Cahuzac, dal canto suo, ha ammesso le proprie responsabilità dopo il k.o., soprattutto per le scelte effettuate nell’intervallo: "Ho esitato tra chiudere la partita sul 3-0 o inserire energie nuove. Dieci minuti dopo eravamo sul 6-0. Mi è mancata lucidità". Ora il Lens cerca una reazione immediata contro il Marsiglia.

#2297. James Morrison issues firm statement over West Brom star’s Premier League potential

West Brom missed the opportunity to move into the Championship’s top two after being held to a 1-1 draw by Southampton on Tuesday night. James Morrison’s side now turn their attention to this weekend’s clash with Millwall, with the Baggies preparing to host the Lions in a bid to strengthen their promotion push.

Ahead of this weekend’s clash, Morrison has been asked about his midfielder Ousmane Diakite, who has been in excellent form this season. The WBA boss was questioned on Diakite’s potential, specifically whether he is a Premier League player in the making, as reported by Steve Tudgay.

Morrison issued a confident response, saying, “Yes he is. With us!” His comments back up those of former WBA man Paul Robinson, who was full of praise for Diakite when speaking to the Express and Star.

“He has Premier League attributes, when you look at him,” the former defender said. “It’s about consistency. We have seen Diakite in the Blackburn game, then the Preston game and now he has gone to that next level against West Ham.

“He stood out against players who are expected to win the league. He destroyed them in the middle of the park.”

Diakite has made an encouraging start to the 2026/27 Championship season, establishing himself as an important figure in the WBA midfield. The 26-year-old has featured in 14 league games, making 11 starts and averaging a FotMob rating of 7.22.

His biggest strengths have been his defensive contributions, recording 25 tackles, 19 interceptions and 58 recoveries. He has also won 81.8% of his aerial duels, placing him in the 95th percentile among midfielders.

Diakite has shown composure in possession too, completing 88.5% of his 544 passes, including 86 line-breaking efforts. His recent performances have been particularly impressive, earning ratings of 7.8 against Southampton and Blackburn and 7.7 against Preston.

Although he is yet to register a goal or assist, his physicality, defensive reliability and ability to retain possession have made him a valuable asset for West Brom.

Morrison is building an exciting, well-balanced midfield at West Brom, combining defensive strength, energy and attacking quality. Diakite and Jayson Molumby provide a solid foundation, while summer signing Felix Horn Myhre adds creativity and goals.

Isaac Price has also emerged as a key attacking threat, already contributing five goals this season. There is promising young talent in Rabby Nzingoula, Toby Collyer and Harry Whitwell, too, giving Morrison options for the future.

The group has helped West Brom make a strong start to the campaign, with its blend of experience and potential offering encouragement for a sustained promotion push.

#2295. Sean Neave won Wolves the game against Bristol City, and he can do the same vs Wrexham: Opinion

Wolves staged an impressive fightback to defeat Bristol City on Wednesday night. Scott Twine put the Robins ahead, and it appeared they would be securing a valuable three-points against an in-form Wanderers side.

However, Cesar Peixoto would introduce Sean Neave in place of Fer Lopez on the 73rd minute, and the move changed the game. First, the Newcastle United loanee pounced on the rebound to level the scores, before meeting Ladislav Krejci’s header back across the box to slide home and win it for the Old Gold with a minute to spare.

The goals were Neave’s first and second for Wolves, and in senior football. The 19-year-old has offered some bright performances off the bench to date, and the traits that helped him win the Bristol City game could see him play a key role against Wrexham this Saturday too.

Neave is a raw and developing young striker. He had only played two times for Newcastle United’s first-team before joining Wolves, but a prolific record at U21 (19 goals in 36 games) and U18 (21 goals in 37 games) means he comes highly regarded from his parent club.

For the most part, Neave has been used as an impact player from the bench. He started and assisted in the win over Burnley and played the first 45 minutes against Portsmouth in the following game.

However, at the moment, it feels he is most effective as an impact sub, and the Bristol City game showed that. Having watched Neave closely at both Wolves and in his Newcastle United development, he has a great burst of pace to pounce on loose balls and run beyond the defensive line.

This was on show in his goals against Bristol City – first pouncing on a rebound before meeting a header at the back-post to tap in his second. In between those two goals, he got the jump on the Robins defence to run in behind before guiding an effort wide off the inside of his foot.

Neave’s sharpness, speed and quick movement will be a worry for Wrexham. A lot of their centre-backs are strong, but they do not have the speed to deal with Neave.

By either playing in balls for Neave to run onto or subbing him on late against tired legs, he can expose the lack of pace among the likes of Joe Worrall, Max Cleworth or Dom Hyam.

Game time is key for the development of all young players, and Neave arrived at Wolves knowing he would have to earn his opportunities. Raul Jimenez joined to lead the line, while Adam Armstrong has a fantastic record at Championship level.

However, Jimenez’s injury means Neave has found opportunities, and he’s taking them. The brace against Bristol City will hopefully be an exciting sign of what’s to come from the teenage striker – both over the course of this season and in his career.

Neave will be taking it one step at a time, but just as he did against the Robins, he can be a thorn in the side of Wrexham’s defence with his pace and movement on Saturday.

#2294. Arsenal lose second successive pre-season friendly after parading Guimaraes

Arsenal suffered back-to-back pre-season defeats as they lost 3-2 to Borussia Dortmund in the Emirates Cup on Sunday.

The loss to the German side followed a 3-1 defeat by Real Betis on Wednesday night in Dublin.

Before kick-off, Bruno Guimarães was paraded to the Arsenal faithful after completing his £75m move from Newcastle on Saturday.

But enthusiasm about the Brazilian’s arrival was dampened when Dortmund took the lead at the Emirates inside the opening 10 minutes. Felix Nmecha’s incisive pass into the penalty area was met by the run of Samuele Inácio, who made no mistake with a clinical finish into the far corner.

The Bundesliga side doubled their lead just before the half-hour mark as 18-year-old Greek international Konstantinos Karetsas curled one in from the edge of the box.

Ten minutes after half-time, Arsenal pulled a goal back through Ethan Nwaneri, who scored from Christos Tzolis’s cross. However, moments later Dortmund restored their two-goal advantage as Joane Gadou smashed home from a corner.

Arsenal kept their hopes alive through a Viktor Gyokeres penalty after Tzolis was brought down, but Dortmund were able to hold on to claim victory. After the full-time whistle, there was a penalty shoot-out, which Arsenal won 5-4.

Arsenal still have a number of players to return to action following their World Cup exploits, including Declan Rice, Bukayo Saka and Noni Madueke. England and Real Madrid midfielder Jude Bellingham was in attendance at the Emirates to watch his younger brother Jobe in action for Dortmund.

As well as Guimarães, there was another addition on display at the Emirates: Arsenal have added last year’s title win to the list of honours displayed inside the stadium.

Arsenal play Cesc Fàbregas’s Como on Wednesday, before taking on Manchester City in the Community Shield next weekend. Mikel Arteta’s side then start the defence of their Premier League crown at home against newly promoted Coventry City.

Arsenal took the Premier League trophy with them to Dublin on Wednesday night and it is on show today at the Emirates. They kick off the defence of their crown on opening night against newly-promoted Coventry is just under two weeks.

Counting down until kick-off ⏰ Make sure you're in your seats by 1.45pm (UK), Gooners - we have a very special guest in store 👀

When Bruno Guimarães signed for Newcastle United from Lyon in January 2022, much was made of the idea that he had rejected Arsenal in favour of a move to St James’ Park. The Brazilian played along with it, posting a video on his social media pages which featured multiple newspaper headlines about Arsenal wanting to buy him.

The Newcastle fans, still delirious about the recent takeover by Saudi Arabia’s Public Investment Fund, understandably lapped it up. Their dream was to overthrow the established powers in English football, and the capture of Guimarães was regarded as a sign that they were truly ready to challenge teams of Arsenal’s stature and wealth.

Even the lyrics to the midfielder’s chant referred to the north Londoners, as Newcastle fans sang that Guimarães “could have signed for Arsenal” but chose instead to play “in the famous black and white”.

Yesterday Arsenal announced the signing of Brazilian midfielder Bruno Guimaraes from Newcastle for £75m on a four-year deal, with the option of a further year. Here is what the Brazilian had to say:

“I feel good, I feel amazing. I am glad for the opportunity. Since the first time I spoke to (Andrea) Berta and Mikel (Arteta) I was excited. I just felt inside of me that I need a new challenge in my life and I think to be an Arsenal player is going to be an exciting challenge in my life.

“I think after playing two World Cups, I am at the point of my life, I think I need a challenge like this. I want to win trophies, I want to make history and I think I am in the right place to do it. I am so excited to start. We have tough games to play this season so the squad is going to be very, very important and I am hopeful we can achieve our dreams.”
