Checking in for the Emirates Cup 🛬 pic.twitter.com/mQ1eAESKHY

Premier League champions Arsenal continue their preparations for the new season as they take on Borussia Dortmund in the Emirates Cup. Arsenal beat Spanish side Athletic Bilbao 3-0 in the 2025 edition, with goals from Viktor Gyokeres, Bukayo Saka and Kai Havertz sealing victory. These sides met during the 2014/15 Champions League group stages, with both games being won 2-0 by the home side.

Arsenal beat Girona 4-1 just over a week ago but fell to a 3-1 defeat to Real Betis in Dublin on Wednesday night. Reflecting on that defeat, Mikel Arteta expressed his frustration at the way his side conceded the goals.

“I am disappointed with the result,” Arteta told Arsenal’s in-house media. “We are here to win football matches. We had some fantastic moments in the first half, the way we played, the way we opened them up. We have to score first, clearly, in the two chances that we had before the score.

“I am very disappointed in the manner that we conceded the first goal, the two giveaways leading to the other two goals. It is true that the finishing quality from them was exceptional, but to win a football match, you have to compete at a different level, especially in those areas of the pitch and that is the learning from today [last Wednesday].”

This is in fact Arsenal’s first game at the Emirates since being crowned Premier League champions, as they sealed the title with a game to spare, with that final match of last season being away at Crystal Palace. After today, Arsenal have two more games, including the Community Shield in a week’s time, before they kick off the defence of their Premier League crown at home against newly-promoted Coventry. The likes of Declan Rice, Bukayo Saka and Martin Zubimendi are still yet to return after their World Cup exploits.

Dortmund, led by Niko Kovac, finished second in the Bundesliga last season, 16 points off winners Bayern Munich. They have played four pre-season friendlies thus far, winning twice and losing twice. The likes of Karim Adeyemi, Julian Brandt and Niklas Sule have left the club this summer.

#2293. Raul Jimenez reveals major four-word update that Wolves fans will love

Raul Jimenez has shared a fresh update on his ongoing recovery from an MCL injury, which will provide a serious boost to Wolves fans as the Mexican striker's return to action slowly but surely edges closer.

The good times are rolling right now at Wolves, who have ascended to second in the Championship behind league leaders Middlesbrough ahead of Saturday lunchtime's crunch trip to Wrexham following three successive victories over Watford, Cardiff and Bristol City.

It has, of course, relieved some much-needed pressure and scrutiny upon the shoulders of Cesar Peixoto, though the Portuguese boss is still not completely out of the woods and remains searching for the backing of some of the Old Gold faithful since his arrival from Gil Vicente to replace Rob Edwards over the summer.

Nonetheless, Wolves are beginning to truly find their rhythm and that's only made all the more impressive by the continued absence of Jimenez, who begun the campaign in strong form upon his emotional homecoming to the Black Country but has not kicked a ball in anger since September.

Jimenez has, however, revealed a new progression update relating to his recovery update, and the words used will provide delight to supporters, teammates and Peixoto alike as all at Molineux eagerly anticipate the veteran frontman's return.

The 36-year-old has been out of action since before the three-week international back in September after suffering a serious MCL injury 36 minutes into Wolves' 1-0 EFL Cup third-round defeat at Everton, in which many believed he shouldn't have been risked in the first place.

Peixoto, then, was left to rue his selection decision when the 130-cap Mexican international's injury turned out to be more severe than expected, forcing the Championship promotion hopefuls to initiate ligament surgery instead.

Surgery would see Jimenez ruled out of proceedings until January, representing a significant blow for Wolves in the wake of a lightning-fast reintroduction to life at Molineux after lighting up the World Cup with co-hosts Mexico by scoring three goals from four matches upon leaving Premier League outfit Fulham.

Wolves' patchy form following the international window amid defeats to both Middlesbrough and Bolton Wanderers ensured that his absence was sorely-felt, although others have stepped up to the mantle in recent weeks to allow the ex-Benfica and Fulham striker to focus entirely on his recovery process instead of rushing to get back sooner rather than later.

The experienced striker recorded his 500th career appearance against Bristol City on Wednesday

His latest update is, however, an extremely positive one, and it may leave the Old Gold faithful optimistic of an even earlier return to the pitch.

Jimenez has kept supporters in the loop with many social media posts and updates throughout his time on the treatment table, which will only be appreciated by the club's fans as they await his return.

He's been stepping up his recovery process in recent weeks, moving on to more intensive physical work as his condition continues to improve.

Jimenez has generally appeared to be in good spirits, too, and that's evidenced by his latest update.

Speaking via his Instagram story on Thursday, Jimenez wrote: "Making really good process 🔥💪🐺."

Just four words were required for an admission that will leave Wolves supporters delighted, with the caption accompanying a video of the goal-getting ace undertaking leg exercises in a bid to regain his strength and mobility.

Top league scorers in the regular season, per season since the second tier became the Championship in 2004–05.

It was either side of a series of stories posted to show Jimenez going through his paces with some arduous gym work, and he's certainly not prepared to let up or allow this injury to get the better of him as he continues to work harder and harder ahead of a return early in the new year.

Jimenez's fellow frontmen at Wolves have begun to find the back of the net with increasing regularity in recent times, and he'll only be hoping that trend continues until he's back on the pitch himself.

One of the main worries when Jimenez suffered his injury was about exactly who was going to come to the fore and provide the cutting-edge in the final third to compensate for his long-term absence after kicking off his second stint at Wolves with three goals from six Championship fixtures.

Thankfully, both Adam Armstrong and Sean Neave are finding form to ensure that Jimenez's absence doesn't end up derailing Wolves' campaign.

Armstrong, who has largely struggled for form since arriving from Southampton in February, looks to have rediscovered his goalscoring boots - which are esteemed at this level, of course - after recording his first strikes since August in back-to-back wins against Watford and Cardiff.

He wasn't on the scoresheet during Wolves' most recent midweek victory at Bristol City, where Newcastle United loanee Neave instead stepped up to the billing by appearing from the bench to score his first professional goals in style with a late brace in the final 10 minutes to seal a dramatic 2-1 away victory for the white-hot visitors.

The pair must not let up, and will each have plenty of opportunities to continue stamping down their authority before Jimenez makes his way back, but Wolves' upturn in form alongside the attacker's striking counterparts providing the goalscoring goods and a key four-word recovery update from the man himself means that Wolves fans have much to feel positive about right now.

#2292. £300k Birmingham City man faces exit: Bolton Wanderers & Oxford United must react

As the winter months roll on, it won't be long until we're looking at how clubs across the EFL can improve their squads in the January transfer window.

Birmingham City will be looking to strengthen for what they hope will be a push towards securing a top-two finish and a place in the Premier League for the first time in 16 years.

But Chris Davies' side does have some deadwood that they'll need to rid themselves of, too, none more so than Scott Wright, who really hasn't settled at Birmingham at all since making the switch from Scottish giants Rangers in the summer of 2024 for around £300,000.

The 29-year-old has made just 24 appearances in the nearly two-and-a-half years he's spent at St Andrew's and was loaned out last January back to Scotland to see out the season with Dundee FC.

With just one one-minute cameo appearance in their EFL Cup to his name this season, he'll surely be angling for more game time, and Birmingham should be in a position to grant that.

Wright's career is a peculiar one. The former youth Scotland international was on the books at Rangers for two-and-a-half years, but never during his time at Ibrox was he a certified starter.

The winger was a very solid squad player during his time with the Gers, and a move south of the border for the first time in his career to a side expected to dominate League One in 2024/25 presented a good opportunity for him to take a more important role elsewhere.

That never came to fruition at Birmingham, though. Despite scoring a late winner on his debut against Wigan Athletic, injuries stopped Wright from playing much of a part in Chris Davies' side's record-breaking campaign.

Through AI, we'll rank the top 25 most intimidating football stadiums in British football, but which stadium comes out on top?

He played just 437 minutes of League One action, and a knee injury in just his third league start for the club in February 2025 not only curtailed his season, but also saw him out of the picture for the first half of last year's Championship return.

At that point, Birmingham had invested even more into the squad, and even though he did get off the bench for a few games back in January, it was always going to be a struggle to get into starting contention, especially as he wasn't while the Blues were in League One.

Wright needs a fresh start, and these two EFL sides should be on hand to give it to him.

Bolton Wanderers have had a fine start to life back in the Championship after a seven-year exile, sitting 17th with 17 points from 14 games and an away record which is amongst the best in the division.

Not many expected the Trotters to pack such a punch going forward, especially given the amount of quality they lost in those areas in the summer due to loanees returning to their parent club and the number of perceived top players they lost out on during the off-season.

Steven Schumacher has done well with the tools he's had, but it's evident that he still wants more, especially in the wide areas. Against Charlton Athletic on Tuesday night, he opted for Max Lott on the left wing, a 19-year-old they signed from Aston Villa's academy in October, for his first senior start for the club.

Some saw that as a potential cry for some reinforcements in January. Thierry Gale aside, the Trotters don't look stunning on the wings. Sam Illing-Junior hasn't excelled, and Kyliane Dong hasn't done much to nail down a starting spot.

Additionally, both of those wingers are on loan at Bolton, so they won't be theirs in the future.

If Bolton can agree on a minimal deal to bring Wright to the Toughsheet Stadium, they'll have a permanent player entering their prime and with a big point to prove that he's good enough to play regular Championship games after not getting a sniff at Birmingham.

The likelihood is, though, that if Wright does remain in the EFL in January, he may find himself dropping down to the top of League One.

If Oxford United's transfer embargo, which forbade them from investing in Aaron Ramsey's squad this past summer, is lifted by January, as the higher-ups at the Kassam Stadium are confident it will be, they should have an eye on Wright to increase their attacking depth.

The Yellows will be in for a busy winter window if the embargo is lifted. Oxford have started the campaign brilliantly under Ramsey, who has taken to his first senior managerial role very well. After their win over Reading on Wednesday, they sit third in League One, three points off the top and with a game in hand.

However, there are some worries about the lack of senior depth in the squad. Ramsey has been brilliant at utilising players from the academy, and it's led to 18-year-old Mo Missanga being one of the more exciting stars in the division.

But if they can bring in some experience out wide, that would be optimal, especially with Villa loanee Luka Lynch only at the club until January.

Wright would automatically be one of the more veteran attacking players in the Oxford side if they moved for him, and he could provide a good boost, if given the right minutes, as the Yellows look to maintain promotion form.

#2291. Huddersfield Town and Middlesbrough won’t believe what's happening at non-league side

It's not uncommon for ex-EFL stars to wind down their careers in non-league, but what's happening at Harborough Town, especially, will catch the eye of Middlesbrough, Huddersfield Town and neutral fans, too.

Harborough are one of the fastest-rising non-league sides right now, having only just joined Step 6 in the non-league ladder in 2010. This season, they're competing in the National League North (Step 2) for the first time in their history, after winning the Southern League Premier Central last year.

With the likes of Morecambe, Macclesfield FC and Hereford now competing with them in league action, the need for extra quality and some ex-EFL professionals was needed in the summer, and continues to be needed as the year goes on.

And that led Mitch Austin's side to form quite the peculiar strike partnership.

Harborough hit headlines ahead of their first season at National League North level by securing the arrival of Britt Assombalonga.

The 33-year-old is still technically Middlesbrough's record signing, having arrived from Nottingham Forest for £15 million back in the summer of 2017. However, Will Lankshear will be set to break that once the add-ons in his move from Tottenham Hotspur are activated.

It's safe to say that Lankshear will probably be better remembered in a Boro shirt, but Assombalonga wasn't exactly a dud. He scored double figures in three of his four Championship campaigns at the Riverside, including 15 in his debut year to help his side reach the play-offs.

After leaving Teesside in 2021, the 33-year-old bounced around Turkish clubs, in between a short stint at Watford, before playing a small part in Barnet's season last year in League Two, and has now dropped a couple more divisions to play for Harborough.

Now, though, he has some competition up top in the form of Kyle Hudlin, the former Huddersfield man and widely recognised as the tallest outfield player in England, standing at six-foot-nine.

Football League World ranks all 24 Championship clubs by the longest-serving member of their squad.

Hudlin signed for Huddersfield off the back of scoring in the National League play-off final for Solihull Moors in 2022. He made just 10 appearances for the Terriers, spending the majority of his time out on loan, including a stint at Newport County, where he scored six in 27 League Two appearances in 2024/25.

After a year in Vietnam, he's returned to England and has signed for Harborough.

Harborough have been on quite the meteoric rise, with four promotions in 15 years, the final three of which have come in the last five seasons.

Their first in the National League North has been steady, with Mitch Austin's side sitting two points above the bottom four in what is turning out to be yet another very competitive Step 2 campaign, after last season saw two sides relegated on 50 or more points.

Assombalonga has been in and out of the side, owing to a few niggling injuries, but has two goals and two assists in six league starts this season.

Harborough do have a small excuse for a slower start than in previous years, though, as they've gone through three qualifying rounds in the FA Cup to reach the First Round proper for just the second time in their history, and for the second time in three years.

Those who remember them from two years ago will know that they took League One side Reading all the way at the Select Car Leasing Stadium in the second round, losing in extra time after drawing 3-3 with them after 90 minutes.

This season, they travel to another third-tier side in the first round, with Mitch Austin's men heading to Leyton Orient. They'll be wanting to cause another scare, and even follow through on upsetting the O's this weekend.

It's not only Assombalonga and Hudlin who have EFL reps under their belts in this Harborough side. Behind them is former three-time League One champion Michael Jacobs, who has over 450 appearances in the Football League to his name and was playing at Northampton Town last year.

It's those players who will be called upon by the Bees to help them through difficult matchups, not only in the league against ex-league sides, but also in FA Cup bouts against teams three divisions higher.

Do you think Harborough Town could cause an FA Cup upset this weekend? Let us know in the comments.

With the amount of ex-EFL scoring power in the squad now, there'll be an expectation for the club to start firing more in the National League North, of which their 13 goals are a divisional low.

As and when they do, Huddersfield and Middlesbrough fans may keep a small glancing eye on whether it's either of their former pro players doing so. And if they both combine against Leyton Orient on Saturday, it's sure to get some traction amongst footballing circles, too.

Manchester United and Tottenham Hotspur return from the international break with plenty to prove when they meet at Old Trafford on Saturday.

Michael Carrick’s side have won just once in their last four domestic matches since beating Ipswich 5-2. A Carabao Cup defeat to Brighton was followed by a loss to 10-man Manchester City, before Manchester United drew with Everton and Fulham. Matheus Cunha’s late equaliser at Fulham rescued a point, but Manchester United still sit 12th.

Roberto De Zerbi’s side are bottom of the Premier League with only two points from their opening five games. Spurs have failed to score in four of those matches, while defeats against Aston Villa and Newcastle have added to the pressure.

Michael Carrick has named his starting eleven for the clash at Old Trafford, with Kobbie Mainoo and Youri Tielemans partnering in midfield as United look to get their season back on track.

Senne Lammens starts in goal, with Leny Yoro, Lisandro Martinez, Harry Maguire and Luke Shaw forming the back four. Carrick has opted for Mainoo and Tielemans in the middle, with Amad Diallo, Bruno Fernandes and Matheus Cunha tasked with supporting Bryan Mbeumo up front.

Leny Yoro had a brilliant international break where he made his debut for France. His performance on debut was enough for Zidane to praise the debutant.

Amad, after missing the start of the season with an injury, returns straightaway to the team.

Carlos Baleba starts on the bench and is expected to make his debut today. Marcus Rashford is also on the bench alongside Shea Lacey.

Lammens; Yoro, Martinez, Maguire, Shaw; Mainoo, Tielemans; Amad, Bruno Fernandes, Mbeumo; Cunha.

Mbeumo gets his place through the middle, while Amad and Cunha start on the wing after scoring the late equaliser against Fulham. Fernandes will be expected to start producing the numbers from last season after a lackluster start to the season, except against Ipswich, before the international break.

United need a response after a difficult run, but Tottenham arrive under pressure of their own. With both sides desperate for points, the starting lineups set the stage for an important afternoon at Old Trafford.

#2289. Manchester United player ratings: United failed to win despite advantage

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United and Tottenham Hotspur returned from the international break with plenty to prove after a disastrous start to the season.

United arrived with just one win, while Tottenham came to Old Trafford without a win.

Both teams had half-chances in the first half but little to show for them. Tottenham had the better chances with chances for Dominic Solanke, Connor Gallagher and Kudus, but none could take advantage of them.

Manchester United came close in the first half to score after Bryan Mbeumo raced towards the goal on a counter-attack to find Amad. He had a difficult shot, and eventually the shot was too weak to cause any damage. The first-half display was met by boos from the Old Trafford crowd at half-time and during the first half as well.

Solanke had another chance at the start of the second half but put it over the top, amid a wrestle with Lisandro Martinez in the six-yard box. It was United, however, who started the second half with promise.

Substitutions by Michael Carrick around the hour mark made the difference as United showed intent before Mbeumo got the goal off Cunha’s low cross. Luke Shaw was at the end of a scuffle with Bentancur, who was shown a red card for headbutting Shaw.

United failed to capitalize on their one-man advantage and seemed to sit on their 1-0 lead, but Solanke punished them with a header to level the game in injury time. The draw left United with just 6 points off their first six games. The result leaves United with their worst start in 40 years.

#2288. Michael Carrick reacts to Man Utd boos after Tottenham draw

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Michael Carrick admitted Manchester United’s 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur was a painful result, after his side failed to protect the lead against 10-man Tottenham at Old Trafford.

Manchester United looked set to claim three points when Bryan Mbeumo scored in the second half off Cunha’s low cross, but Dominic Solanke’s late equaliser in injury time left Carrick facing more questions on United’s performance and his future.

Speaking to BBC Match of the Day, the United manager felt his players improved after the break but failed to handle the situation when victory was within reach.

“It was a mixed game. We found it tough to start with. For a number of reasons, we were not quite there. You could see it was a challenging time,” Carrick said.

“But the lads grew into the game, in the second half I thought we were better in everything. To get the game as we wanted it, in such a good position – we should see it through. We have to manage those situations.”

United have now conceded two late goals that have cost them valuable points, something Carrick believes has made their position look worse than it might have otherwise.

Asked whether a lack of confidence had contributed to United’s slow start, he disagreed but admitted matches can quickly slip away from his side.

The boos at half-time and full-time also clearly affected him.

“It’s not nice, it hurts a lot because I care about this club. But I understand the disappointment, the reality is what it is, we need to get back to winning games.”

#2287. Bryan Mbeumo reacts to Man Utd draw with Tottenham

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Bryan Mbeumo admitted Manchester United have work to do after their frustrating 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur, despite scoring a superb second-half goal at Old Trafford.

The Cameroon international put Manchester United ahead in the 73rd minute, finishing in the far corner after Matheus Cunha created the opportunity from a low cross. However, Dominic Solanke’s stoppage-time equaliser denied Michael Carrick’s side a much-needed victory, even though Tottenham had been reduced to 10 men following Rodrigo Bentancur’s red card.

Manchester United have failed to win any of their last three Premier League games against sides that have received a red card (D1 L2).

3 – Manchester United have failed to win any of their last three Premier League games against sides that have received a red card (D1 L2). Disadvantage? pic.twitter.com/DuSpvymZfu

Speaking to Sky Sports after the game, Mbeumo acknowledged that United had taken too long to find their rhythm.

“We took time to get into the game, but we played well in the second half. In the first half, that wasn’t us, we had to change. We tried to get at them,” he said.

Mbeumo recognised the threat Tottenham posed and admitted the result was disappointing, particularly given United’s position in the game before Solanke’s late goal.

“We know Spurs have good players who can be dangerous. It’s a bit disappointing, we need to work on things.”

United have struggled for consistency in the opening weeks of the season, and dropping points after taking the lead will do little to ease the pressure on Carrick. Mbeumo, however, insisted the team must look ahead rather than dwell on the setback.

“We need to keep going forward, as we have a lot of games.”

Michael Carrick was pleased with Mbeumo’s contribution, highlighting both the quality of his finish and the different roles he has played since joining United.

“Bryan’s done great. It was a fantastic finish again; he’s obviously played different roles for us but we know what he offers,” the United manager said.

“He’s got good quality and obviously the more goals he scores, the better. He’s doing really well for us.”

Mbeumo’s goal was one of the few bright moments in another difficult afternoon for United. Carrick’s team must now find a way to turn things around from here after just six points from six games.

#2286. Birmingham City fans will love Jude Bellingham's reaction to England news

Jude and Jobe Bellingham have received call-ups to the England senior squad under Thomas Tuchel for the first time as a duo, with the former paying tribute to their early-career spells at Birmingham City.

The Premier League and Championship will halt their respective campaigns after the upcoming weekend's round of fixtures, with the Three Lions in Nations League action against Croatia and Spain.

It has been a relatively successful year for England on the footballing stage, with a third-placed finish at the FIFA World Cup hosted in the United States, Canada and Mexico being the nation's best in the tournament since winning it in 1966.

Jude Bellingham was an integral part of the side's run to the semi-finals and has continued to impress in the four Nations League matches during the previous international window.

Many of the high-profile names at Tuchel's disposal can credit spells at various EFL clubs for aiding their eventual development, including Ballon d'Or winner, Harry Kane.

Unsurprisingly, Bellingham was quick to reference his boyhood club yet again in a proud family milestone.

A reunion with Chris Wood has been debated amongst Bluenoses ahead of the January window

Jude and Jobe Bellingham are the most high-profile names to have graduated from Birmingham's Wast Hills academy setup this century, with the former becoming the club's youngest-ever player and goalscorer seven years ago before initially moving to Borussia Dortmund.

The West Midlands outfit then opted to retire the '22' shirt to help create a legacy, and that has only grown stronger through the 23-year-old's current exploits with Real Madrid and England.

Jobe, 21, played 26 times for Blues between 2022 and 2023 before moving to Sunderland for £3m, and after the Black Cats' Premier League return, also signed for Dortmund in a £27.8m deal.

He has since shone for the Bundesliga giants, and after impressing domestically and in the UEFA Champions League, the 12-time Under-21s international earned his first senior Three Lions call-up on Friday.

Such feats were marked by his older brother on social media, who posted a picture of them sporting Birmingham colours during their academy days.

During the early years of his career, Jobe has often been compared to his older brother, despite rightfully wanting to create his own story on the pitch.

That has clearly been the case given his rave reviews at Dortmund, whilst he also racked up over 100 Championship appearances for Birmingham and Sunderland.

Like Jude, the Stourbridge native has never lost sight of the impact Blues staff had in order for him to flourish at such a young age.

“I’m so grateful for everything they’ve (Birmingham) done for me over many years, since I was really young," he said shortly after leaving B9.

Blues fans, how proud are you of Jude and Jobe Bellingham's exploits? Comment below!

“At Birmingham, a lot of the academy coaches helped loads, just with my understanding of the game. I think they’ve made me a better person, I believe. There’s also a lot of players in the first team who helped me grow into the first-team environment.

Whilst they have their own career paths to focus on, both Jude and Jobe will be loving the developments at St. Andrew's @ Knighthead Park right now, which is a stark contrast to how the club was run during their respective breakthrough years.

Knighthead's plans for the Sports Quarter and Powerhouse stadium have seen Jude involved in promotional videos, whilst he was also present at the pre-season friendly against FC Barcelona.

On the pitch, Chris Davies' side are doing their utmost to end a long-standing Premier League exile, as Blues sit third in the Championship having lost just two of their first 14 games.

Joyous feelings will be reciprocated by those associated with the club for the progress made by the duo, who, in turn, will hope to see the club back in the top-flight very soon.

Everton took the lead through Thierno Barry after a defensive error from Sven Botman, before Iliman Ndiaye scored a penalty that was won cheaply by summer signing Hayden Hackney.

Newcastle’s raft of half-time changes helped David Moyes’s team strengthen their grip further, with Tyrique George adding a stylish third and 19-year-old Harrison Armstrong providing his second assist of the game.

#2284. Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp tear into Man Utd after Tottenham draw

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United have come under fresh criticism after their disappointing 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur, with Sky Sports pundits Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp questioning the team’s performance and the challenges facing Michael Carrick.

Manchester United looked on course for a much-needed victory at Old Trafford after Bryan Mbeumo put them ahead in the second half. However, despite Tottenham being reduced to 10 men, Dominic Solanke’s late equaliser denied Carrick’s side all three points.

Gary Neville felt Tottenham deserved the points, pointing to Solanke’s late goal and his impact as Tottenham captain.

“Tottenham deserved a point. That was a really important moment for Solanke. He’d missed some big chances and was captain for the first time,” Neville said on Sky Sports.

“They were the better team by far. If they’d left without a point, it would have been terrible for them.”

The former United defender also expressed concern about what the result means for Carrick, who faces increasing pressure to improve performances and results.

“Michael Carrick is left with a lot to think about. It was a worrying day in terms of the level of performance and a big week ahead.”

Manchester United spent massively in the summer to overhaul their midfield. However, Jamie Redknapp was critical of the performance he saw on the pitch.

“I do not see enough connections in that midfield, and they look devoid of any confidence, ideas, or patterns of play, playing out the back,” Redknapp said.

“Tottenham were just much better than them in every department, but that is a theme this season.”

The former Liverpool midfielder also questioned United’s energy and attacking organization, suggesting the squad is not strong enough to meet the demands placed upon it.

“Their energy levels are not good enough, and they look loose going forward; there was no real structure.”

Redknapp added that Carrick cannot fix every issue alone, insisting the manager needs backing to improve the squad.

“The squad is not good or strong enough, and Michael [Carrick] needs help in that respect.”

#2283. Bailey Cadamarteri needs to escape Wrexham: Portsmouth & Plymouth must act

But the forward has struggled for consistent game time since signing for the Red Dragons, making just four appearances in the Championship so far this campaign.

The 21-year-old has yet to make a start in the league for the Welsh outfit, as he approaches a full year since making the switch from Hillsborough.

His lack of minutes under Phil Parkinson has prompted a lot of speculation surrounding his immediate future at the Racecourse Ground, with his Dad even hitting out after defeat to Sheffield United.

With the January window now quickly approaching, it’s becoming increasingly apparent that a temporary, or perhaps even permanent, exit could be on the cards in the new year.

Here we look at two clubs who must act if Cadamarteri becomes available in the January window…

Portsmouth have scored the fewest number of goals in the top half of the Championship table after the first 14 games.

John Mousinho’s side have found the back of the net on only 19 occasions, and Marko Milovanovic’s injury in their 3-1 midweek win against Swansea City only raises further concerns regarding their attacking options.

The Pompey boss has shown that he can work well with younger players, and someone like Cadamarteri could be a smart option to bolster their ranks in attack.

With the success of Pepple, and previously Lorent Tolaj, working under the Plymouth boss could be an exciting opportunity to improve as a player, and receive regular game time.

It is vitally important for the 21-year-old that he prioritises consistent minutes with any potential January exit, and a switch to League One might be the step he needs to take in order to guarantee that.

#2282. ‘Embarrassing themselves!’ Jamie Carragher hits back at Rio Ferdinand over Cristiano Ronaldo defence

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Jamie Carragher has taken aim at Rio Ferdinand for defending Cristiano Ronaldo following the Portuguese forward’s latest controversy with his national team.

Ronaldo has found himself at the centre of a growing dispute after abruptly leaving Portugal’s national team camp following a reported disagreement with head coach Jorge Jesus. The incident has prompted strong reactions from former teammates and pundits, with Ferdinand firmly siding with the Al-Nassr star.

Jamie Carragher, however, believes Ronaldo’s reputation should not shield him from criticism when his behaviour becomes difficult to defend.

Rio Ferdinand, who played alongside Cristiano Ronaldo at Manchester United between 2003 and 2009, criticised Jorge Jesus for the way he handled the situation.

Speaking on his Rio Presents podcast, the former United captain criticised Jesus for not showing respect to Cristiano Ronaldo.

“Cristiano Ronaldo was put in a very bad situation and was treated in an inappropriate way. He has an enormous history in football. Jorge Jesus did not deal with Ronaldo well and chose to treat him in that way, but I cannot find any explanation for it,” Ferdinand said.

Despite Jesus being Portugal’s manager, Ferdinand questioned his standing, comparing him with the likes of Sir Alex Ferguson and Pep Guardiola. To say the least, Ferdinand only embarrassed himself doing that.

Jamie Carragher saw the argument very differently and rightfully so.

Speaking on The Overlap Daily, the former Liverpool defender suggested that Ferdinand’s friendship with Ronaldo was influencing his reaction to the incident.

“People think it’s good to be close to people in the game, but this is where it becomes a problem,” Carragher explained.

He then called out both Ferdinand and broadcaster Piers Morgan, who has also been vocal in his support of Ronaldo.

“People like Piers Morgan and Rio Ferdinand, who are big people in the media whether you like them or not, are embarrassing themselves with the nonsense that they’re coming out with because they know Ronaldo. You can know someone and try to look after them, but you cannot defend the indefensible.”

Carragher believes Ronaldo’s achievements should not put the forward beyond criticism, particularly when his actions involve his national team and its management.

Gary Neville, who also played with Cristiano Ronaldo, has questioned Ronaldo’s handling of the situation, despite his longstanding affection for his former Manchester United teammate.

Neville argued that Ronaldo’s public response had made matters worse and the forward needed to accept criticism rather than expect others to defend him.

“With Cristiano, it’s difficult because I love him, but I can’t watch what happened at United in his second period and then see him make a statement like that without saying something. He has to be pulled up on that and told that it isn’t acceptable,” Neville said.

He also suggested Ronaldo’s extraordinary success, wealth and influence could have contributed to a sense that he was beyond challenge.

“When you have a billion pounds in the bank, the achievements he has had and the power he has, you feel you’re unbreakable, you feel you’re unstoppable,” Neville added.

Despite his criticism, Neville does not believe the episode will define Ronaldo’s legacy permanently. He expects the attention to fade once the forward retires, allowing his achievements on the pitch to take centre stage again.

#2281. Spurs have one Preston North End transfer clause that already looks doomed

Preston North End's 12-season stay in the Championship is in serious jeopardy based on their early season form.

The Lilywhites were long-term stayers in the second tier the last time they were here, keeping themselves in from 2000 until 2011 with some near promotion misses along the way, before dropping into League One.

Since 2015, PNE have been back in the Championship, mostly being a mid-table team, albeit with some brief flirtations with the top six and also the drop zone, and there was hope over the summer that a switch in transfer business would unlock a new level for them.

North End are usually quite frugal with their transfer budget, cherry-picking some younh, high-quality Premier League loanees and opting to recruit permanent players on cheaper fees or free transfers.

There was much evidence of that last summer when Alfie Devine, Lewis Dobbin, Daniel Jebbison and Harrison Armstrong arrived on loan, whilst they saved paying fees until January of last season, when Odel Offiah, Andrew Moran and Callum Lang all arrived at Deepdale.

Football League World ranks all 24 Championship clubs by the longest-serving member of their squad.

For whatever reason though, that tact changed this past summer, with around £15 million spent on new faces and over £5 million recouped on Milutin Osmajic and Ben Whiteman's sales.

And £6 million of that windfall spent was making the loan signing of Devine a permanent one, which came as a surprise to many North End fans, given the interest surrounding his services.

Celtic, Southampton and Wolves were among the clubs that were keen on taking Devine from Spurs, but even after the initial deadline was missed to seal a deal with the playmaker, PNE were able to convince the 22-year-old that Deepdale was the best place for his future.

Devine was once the youngest player and scorer for Tottenham when he featured in an FA Cup clash with Marine in 2021, but he had to go out on several loans to develop, with stints taken in at Port Vale, Plymouth Argyle and Belgian outfit Westerlo before ending up at PNE.

And with Tottenham being a big-spending club looking to turn around their fortunes under Roberto De Zerbi, there was no room for Devine in his midfield for 2026-27, leading to the decision to let him move on permanently for a decent fee, much like they did with Will Lankshear and Ash Phillips when selling the pair to Middlesbrough.

Spurs hold extra clauses when it comes to Lankshear and Phillips, which means they have a vested interest in their futures and will want Boro in the Premier League, as well as matching rights on the pair, which means they are given the chance to match any accepted bid that comes in for their services - it's not quite like that with Devine, but there's still a sell-on clause in there.

The Daily Mail reported at the time of the deal that Tottenham had negotiated a 20 per cent sell-on clause with PNE for Devine, which is based on any profit the Lancashire outfit end up making on Devine - as it stands though, that could be in jeopardy.

The reason why Devine was so in-demand during the summer was his form for North End, with eight goals scored from midfield and six assists registered.

But he's showed nothing like that kind of ability for PNE in the 2026-27 season, scoring just one goal and notching one assist, with the Lilywhites looking like they're in big relegation trouble already.

Ahead of their 15th match of the Championship season against Southampton, North End have just five points on the board and are nine points from safety, which whilst not confirming relegation by any stretch of the imagination, they'll need to go some way to overturn the form they've showed so far.

Paul Heckingbottom's early sacking and club icon Graham Alexander coming into replace him hasn't helped results whatsoever, and Devine is yet to show what he's really capable of - with the general consensus of the fanbase being that he's missing the link-up with now-Southampton forward Dobbin that he shared last season.

Type the most common nickname next to each of the 24 clubs in the 2026–27 Championship. Leave off “The”. Some popular alternatives count too.

Clubs in the 2026–27 Sky Bet Championship, listed alphabetically.

Undoubtedly there's a lack of quality in the North End squad compared to last season, and that's showing on the pitch - Alexander didn't even turn to Devine in their 2-0 loss to Stoke City when he was sat on the bench, and he had to wait until the second half earlier this week against Sheffield United to come on, assisting Callum Lang's consolation goal in a 3-2 loss.

With the current trajectory that PNE find themselves going in, Devine will likely not be playing in League One next season if PNE find themselves there, but they won't be getting their £6 million back based on this season's performances, no matter how much potential the former Wigan Athletic youth prospect has.

It would also mean that Spurs do not get any more money for Devine despite their 20 per cent sell-on clause, which they will find a shame, as they would've been banking on Devine continuing to prosper at Deepdale - unlike their clauses for Lankshear and Phillips, Devine's clause is one that they can perhaps almost forget about.
